The readings' detached (D272's "about two thirds", 0.62, ours: the books say "detached", no share) and staccato (0.35), now on every entry of the subject: I/2's subject alone with Keller's reading went from 61 ms gaps (1.26: no marks) to 132 on the harpsichord, 79 → 143 on the piano, 52 → 172 on CLASSIC CHIP. The plucked synthesized damper (80 → 30 ms) and a held recording's release done by its written end applied to every note. (I/2's opening without a reading hardly moved: the rule's holds there were D294's.) **Now** (ARTICULATION): the default a natural playing, D294's lengths: a step joined (no gap, no new attack on the sustained sounds: D302's join kept), a third too among notes shorter than a beat (between longer ones let go just before the next, as before D302); a leap of a fourth or more a little shortened (0.88; harpsichord 0.85, piano 0.88: they were 0.8 and 0.85), a repeated note a little more (0.78; 0.75, 0.78: were 0.72 and 0.75); no bass rule. Detached and staccato only where a reading marks them, on every entry of its subject (per reading, so per fugue): detached 0.75 of the value (the owner's 75%), staccato 0.5, the gap at most 0.4 of a beat; such a note (Play.apart) has its release -30 dB at its held end, plucked or held (the damper 30 ms on a plucked synthesized note); every other note ends as before D302 (released at its end, the damper 80 ms). HEAR THE SUBJECT plays them so (readings.ts uses ART_HOLD). A fugue opened without a reading (engine.ts) gets the default only; play.html plays every fugue by a reading, and Book II has readings too (II/22: BODKY's, the first, detaching its subject's first seven notes; RIEMANN's). Measured: II/22's bass alone, 0 gaps on all three sounds again (mean hold 0.991, 0.993, 0.993); I/2's opening 44-46 ms gaps on the harpsichord's leaps (1.26 63-65), 32 ms on the piano's (43); I/2's subject by Keller 119 ms (harpsichord), 136 (piano), 115 (CLASSIC CHIP). **The singers.** VOCAL SYNTH's source was a nearly flat pulse train (n^-0.4) for every voice: through the parallel formants' skirts its harmonics reached 10 kHz, wobbling with the vibrato and the scoops (the glitter), and each glottal pulse was a click above 5 kHz (1,779 in the bass's 20 s alone, the loudest 19 dB over its surroundings): the buzz of a trumpet. Now: the top keeps the bright glottis (the "idol"), the second and the mezzo a softer one (glottalWarm, n^-0.8), the tenor and the bass softer still (glottalLow, n^-1.2: -7 dB an octave; -12 was tried: through the modelled phone speaker the low voices fell 15 dB under the top); a lowpass over each (`Sing.top`, 7 kHz on the top to 3.5 kHz on the bass), the low voices' formants wider (`bw` 1.3), their F3-F5 3-4 dB lower (`upper`), more breath (0.22-0.24), smaller scoops (20-25 cents), no "ring", the d's and r's bursts softer (0.5 → 0.16, 0.12 → 0.05) and ramped in; the mezzo and the tenor sing "a" (ra, na; "o" was tried: 9-12 dB weaker on a phone than on a full-range speaker); levels and SING_GAIN (4.5 → 3.4) by measurement. The share above 3 kHz 2.7% → 0.9% (the bass 3.9% → 0.1%, its centroid 1208 → 584 Hz). SOLO VOICES' clicks were the synthesized plosive: 2 ms of noise at 3.5 kHz on every new syllable of the bass's "da", out of the bass rule's silent gaps (65-68 dB over the gap before it): gone (no synthesized noise on a recording; the d opens from 900 Hz, the lowpass over 50 ms); its scoops smaller (15-35 cents: a recording's rate moves its formants, a chirp of the highs); 14 → 0 such transients in II/22's 20 s. The "tinkling": in I/2 the subject's effect (D213, GLASS SPARK: an FM bell an octave up on its quavers) stood -12 dB under all of SOLO VOICES' highs (-15 in VOCAL SYNTH; under the harpsichord's own, -22): a ping or brass layer is no longer played over a singing voice (subject-fx.ts playFx); the plate darker (8.5 → 6.5 kHz). **PIPE ORGAN**: a trio over the pedal, one clear colour a line: a sweet flute on top (Flöte 8': a sine, a soft octave and twelfth a cent apart, a gentle chiff), a Principal 8' with a quiet 4' (alto), the Trompete (tenor; a solo reed, every harmonic, bright to 5 kHz, a 30 ms blip, its Principal 4' removed; the top of two), the Krummhorn the second of five, the recorded pedal kept; the recorded plenum (Principal to a mixture) withdrawn, the church smaller (RT60 2.8 → 2.2 s, 5.5 kHz) and sent less (0.1-0.14, was 0.14-0.28). **SWITCHED-ON** (after Switched-On Bach): nearly dry (room 0.24 → 0.05), the filters open (4.5-7 times the note, were 1.3-3.4), quick blips, a little drive: a sawtooth lead through a moderately resonant ladder, a hollow square (second of five), a nasal 12% pulse (alto), a "bloop" (a square and a sawtooth, Q 7, a four-fold sweep in 70 ms: tenor), a driven sawtooth bass over a sub-octave square (1.6 kHz floor). **MODULAR** (East and West coast): a hard-synced sawtooth lead (SHAPES.hardSync) through the ladder, a wavefolded sustained voice (SHAPES.folded, second of five), the low-pass gate's "bongo" (the folded wave, the cutoff and level falling together in 0.3 s: alto), an FM bell at 3.5 times the note (the ring modulator's metal) settling to a clear body (tenor), a sequencer's bass (sawtooth over a square, resonant, snapping shut, driven); room 0.12. **FOREST 64 and PARTY 64 withdrawn** (0 each): a stored choice of them or of TEMPLE 64 and AGENT 64 opens on NOCTURNE 32-BIT (RETIRED); their patches, sample sets and rooms deleted (the recordings stay in public/samples). Measured (II/22 54-74 s, integrated LUFS; phone = touch emulated through the 600 Hz high-pass model, each voice alone, S A T B): the eight rendered -20.6 (PIPE ORGAN) to -22.5 (harpsichord); PIPE ORGAN -28.5 -29.0 -28.9 -27.6 (spread 1.4 dB, was 3.5), VOCAL SYNTH -29.5 -27.6 -30.7 -29.3 (3.1, was 5.2), SOLO VOICES 2.4 (bass +1.8), SWITCHED-ON -29.6 -29.8 -33.3 -31.5 (3.7, was 7.6), MODULAR -28.8 -31.2 -31.1 -31.5 (2.7, was 9.2). | D305 | The owner on D304's sounds (WTC 1.29): VOCAL SYNTH "I actually think the more non-detached previous version [1.27] sounded better than the current one (only articulation…); but soprano is too chipmunk-y, can you make it more human? maybe something more like a female voice, or a kid choir singer?"; SOLO VOICES "eliminate that off-putting tremolo… Make these more straight historically accurate performance voices (little to no tremolo [vibrato]…). Check one singer per voice kind of settings of Bach's vocal music… The bass shakes weirdly in the beginning of the excerpt… the notes have very fast decay… I prefer the current version to the previous one with respect to the detached/non-detached bass"; SWITCHED-ON "the bass was less off-putting in the previous detached version. Perhaps it needs to be a little bit less pronounced… Change bass to a more plucky sound, well-rounded but more layered. Try out adding some kind of LFO"; MODULAR "not good. Maybe go all in for the metallic tubey marimba-ey sounds you chose for tenor and alto… Call it Bleeps and Bloops. Take inspiration from Stereolab"; PIPE ORGAN and CLASSIC CHIP kept. **VOCAL SYNTH, articulation** (phrasing.ts style "sung", SUNG; styleOf("vocalSynth")): measured first (II/22 54-74 s, the 79 adjacent pairs of the four voices, no reading): joined (no new attack) 1.27 60/79 (76%), 1.29 71/79 (90%); audible gaps (rendered each voice alone, under -25 dB for 15 ms or more) 1.27 16 (the bass 11 of 19, 151 ms: D302's bass rule), 1.29 5 (70 ms, the leaps): 1.27 was the more detached, not the less, so the owner's "more non-detached" is read both ways: no silences (singers carry the line), and the bass articulated as 1.27's was, by its consonant instead of a gap. Now: a step, a third and a fourth joined (one vowel, the pitch gliding); a leap of a fifth or more, a repeated note and the lowest voice's notes of a third of a beat to a beat a new syllable ("syllable": held to the next onset and over it, the next sung with its consonant and scoop, Play.legato not set); a reading's detached and staccato and a rest as before. Measured: joined 64/79 (81%), syllables 15, gapless 79/79, audible gaps 0. SOLO VOICES keeps "plain" (the owner prefers its bass), SWITCHED-ON gets its own (below); the keyboards' rules untouched. **VOCAL SYNTH, the soprano** ("la"): a grown woman's choral soprano, not the "idol": `size` 1.04 → 0.96 ("a" F1/F2 about 770/1100 Hz, an adult woman's sung /a/; 1.04 moved them toward a child's), the first formant tuned to the pitch where the pitch passes it (`Sing.tune` 1.06: F1 at least 1.06 f0; sopranos raise F1 to f0 above about 400-500 Hz by opening the jaw: Sundberg; Joliveau, Smith and Wolfe 2004), the softer glottis (glottalWarm: a woman's steeper source spectrum; flagged: from general phonetics, Hanson 1997, not checked here), the 3 kHz lift 5 → 2 dB (the singer's-formant cluster is a trained man's and weakens in sopranos at high pitch: Sundberg), the lowpass 7 → 6 kHz, more breath (0.12 → 0.22) and an aspirated onset (`Sing.aspirate`), the scoop 70 → 25 cents, its overshoot 15 → 4, the fall 120 → 60, the vibrato 30 cents from 0.3 s → 12 cents from 0.5 s (6 Hz, ramped in): nearly straight. Measured (the soprano alone, II/22): spectral centroid 946 → 806 Hz, the share above 3 kHz 2.5% → 0.4%, the 2.5-3.5 kHz band against 0.3-1.5 kHz -18.4 → -28.1 dB; pitch wobble on its notes (3-9 Hz, scoops included) 15.2 → 5.0 cents; its level 1.25 → 0.43 (the tuned F1 put its strength on the fundamental: +9 dB). **SOLO VOICES** (sound.ts sing*, sampled.ts, public/samples/solo*): the "tremolo" was the recordings': VocalSet's f2, f5, m11 and m8 sang vibrato on most notes (measured over the 1-3 s loops, the 3-9 Hz band of the pitch: soprano 33 cents mean, 94 at most; alto 18 (68); tenor 14 (44); bass 11 (34)), with the level swinging 0.8-1.7 dB with it, and every note tiled from cuts as short as 0.3 s; the engine added none. The bass's "shake": its D2-A2 (the excerpt opens on G2-A2) were all its C3 shifted down 3-10 semitones by rubberband (their pitch spread 24-30 cents a note, against 8-15 now). Rebuilt (tools/playback/vocalset/build_straight.py, segs2.py; VocalSet CC BY 4.0, credited as before): VocalSet has "straight" takes (long tones, scales, arpeggios), but few pitches each and no man below B2 in them; so per semitone the steadiest of the straight and slow takes of one singer per part (f7, f8, m3, m8: chosen as the steadiest across their range), within 4 semitones (the alto's F3-G3 from C4, 5-7: no woman in VocalSet sings under C4), its pitch held steady (the recording read at a varying rate that undoes its wobble: the formants move by its few cents only), its level held steady, shifted with formants preserved, tiled (120 ms pitch-aligned crossfades) into the same 3.25 s files with the 1.0-3.0 s loop (the chorales use them too); every semitone (S C4-A5, A F3-D5, T B2-A4, B D2-F#4), 96 files, 3.8 MB (were 65, 2.6 MB). Measured: the loops' wobble 0.9 / 0.9 / 1.9 / 2.0 cents (S A T B), level swing 0.6-0.7 dB; rendered (II/22, each voice alone, its notes from 90 ms in) 33.8 / 21.6 / 14.9 / 11.7 → 3.0 / 8.3 / 4.7 / 3.5 cents (the scoops and glides included), the level's spread in a note 4.3 / 2.3 / 2.8 / 2.5 → 1.6 / 1.6 / 1.7 / 1.9 dB. "Very fast decay": no note decayed while held (its level from 100 ms after the onset to 100 ms before the end: a median within ±0.9 dB, before and after); the release was quick (to -20 dB in 59-71 ms): `rec.r` 0.08 → 0.15 (117-122 ms), and a long note's messa di voce (`syllable.messa` 1.5 dB: up to two fifths of a note of 0.9 s or more, then eased; joined, back to its level; from the written length alone); the soprano's scoop 35 → 20 cents; no vibrato added (Baroque one-to-a-part straight tone; Rifkin, Parrott, the Dunedin Consort and Bach Collegium Japan's soloists were the owner's references, not checked here). **SWITCHED-ON** (sound.ts sol*; phrasing.ts style "switched", SWITCHED): the bass plucked, rounder and layered: two sawtooths 8 cents apart, the sub-octave square, a soft triangle an octave up, the ladder's Q 2.5 → 1.4, plucking open five-fold and closing over 80 ms to 1.4 kHz (D291), the level falling to 0.45 (was a held 0.65), less drive (1.2 → 0.6); its notes of a third of a beat to a beat held 0.8 of their value (II/22: 11 of 19 pairs apart, gaps 118 ms; 1.29 none, 1.27 11 of 150 ms). Movement: the lead's ladder swept by a slow LFO with the clock (0.45 Hz, a third of an octave either way: Patch.filter.sweep), the alto's narrow pulse a real PWM (a sawtooth less itself delayed, 12% ± 7% at 0.35 Hz with the clock: Patch.pwm.clock), the bloop a second sawtooth 7 cents up (a slow chorus) and wandering ±0.1 across the stereo (0.23 Hz). The bass -2.5 dB on a phone and on a full-range speaker (against the soprano -4.4 dB on a phone, was -1.9). **BLEEPS AND BLOOPS** (MODULAR remade, its id kept: a stored choice keeps it; after Stereolab's Emperor Tomato Ketchup and Dots and Loops): a vibraphone on top (a sine and its bar's fourth partial, an FM strike at 4 times the note gone in a tenth of a second, a long ring; the motor's tremolo, 5.5 Hz, 35%), a Farfisa-like combo organ (a square, a quarter pulse an octave up, a little sawtooth; a 6.4 Hz vibrato: the sustained line, the second of five and the middle of three), D304's low-pass-gate marimba ("bongo", the owner's alto; a longer ring) and FM tube (the owner's tenor; a 3-cent tape wow at 0.7 Hz), a round Moog-like bass pluck (a sawtooth over a square an octave down, the ladder plucking open and closing over 110 ms to 1.3 kHz, falling to half its level); a small spring (REVERBS.spring: RT60 1.3 s, 400 Hz-4.2 kHz, a 37 ms flutter echo fed back 0.35), sent 0.05-0.22; i18n BIPES E BLOPES (pt), BIPS Y BLOPS (es). Measured (II/22 54-74 s, integrated LUFS; phone = the 600 Hz high-pass model, each voice alone, S A T B; desk, the same full range): VOCAL SYNTH -20.9 → -20.5 (phone -28.7 -27.6 -30.4 -29.3, spread 2.8 dB, the bass against the soprano -0.6), SOLO VOICES -21.3 → -20.7 (-27.2 -29.0 -30.0 -27.1, 2.9, +0.1; the alto and tenor 0.68 and 0.7, were 0.6: the new singers' levels), SWITCHED-ON -21.8 → -22.4 (-29.6 -29.2 -33.2 -34.0, 4.8, -4.4), BLEEPS AND BLOOPS -21.9 → -21.4 (-31.0 -30.4 -31.5 -33.7, 3.3, -2.7); the others as D304 (-20.6 to -22.5). Measured, not heard: whether the soprano sounds like a woman, the straightened singers human (their pitch now steadier than a singer's: about 1-2 cents of wobble left), the syllabic bass sung rather than pecked, the vibraphone a vibraphone; the sources Sundberg and Joliveau et al. checked only through search summaries. | phrasing.ts Style sung/switched, styleOf, SUNG, SWITCHED, Art syllable, phrase (bass), sung(); engine.ts, proto-game.ts (bass); chip.ts singNote (Sing.tune, aspirate), sampledNote (syllable.messa), patchNote pwm.clock, REVERBS.spring; sound.ts Sing tune/aspirate, Patch syllable.messa, pwm.clock, FxKind spring, PATCHES vocLa, sing*, sol*, modVibe/modOrgan/modBongo/modBell/modBass, PRESETS modular (BLEEPS AND BLOOPS); sampled.ts VSCO_CENTS solo*; public/samples/solo*, LICENSES.txt; sample-manifest.ts; tools/playback/vocalset/build_straight.py, segs2.py, README; PlayPop.tsx POP_SOUNDS; i18n pt, es; test/d305-sounds, chip-presets, d297-presets, d302-presets, d302-articulation, d294-sounds, bench-pop-sounds | WTC 1.32 |
| D306 | The owner: "the notes keep ringing in the player's voice even when they let it go. staccato has become impossible", then "why the hell is player's voice playing by itself even if player doesn't do shit? this is completely against the idea of the game." Both came from BLUETOOTH MODE (D299), which on play.html switches itself on (AUTO) when a stored sound delay or the reported latency passes 80 ms, so a delay once set for earbuds kept it on through the speaker. D299 put every one of the player's notes on the schedule at its written length, touched or not, and a touch only scored. **Now:** a touched note is cut as the touch lets go (never before it has sounded 50 ms), so staccato is heard again, a headphones' delay late. The voice is live or silenced per track: a miss or a wrong note silences it, cutting its notes scheduled and not touched; nothing more is scheduled until a right touch, which sounds itself at once (a delay late) and brings the voice back. The player's notes go on the schedule only 0.15 s ahead (ARM_AHEAD_S), so a miss leaves little to cut. A player who touches nothing hears at most the first note. Decision 11's question on a missed note in Bluetooth mode is settled this way. | proto-game.ts (live, silence, voiced, Armed.own/at, Held.armed, ARM_AHEAD_S); test/bt-mode.test.ts, test/bench-bluetooth.test.ts | WTC 1.30 |
| D307 | The owner: "in sidescrolling with staves, the beginning of the piece (with the armadura, tonality, sharps etc and clef), is not sidescrolling, it's static. ugly hell … Gerubach scrolls it all." D298 kept the opening's clefs and key signatures in a fixed column at the left once the opening had gone by, the music passing under it. Now nothing is kept: the clefs, keys and metre scroll away with the opening, as in GeruBach's videos, and the playhead stands 35% into the whole box (it was 35% of the box right of the column). The player's staff stays marked at the left edge in the open score. | sheet-view.ts (draw; the column's painting removed) | WTC 1.31 |
| D308 | Seven owner reports on play.html (iPhone, landscape). (1) "if you're at stage selection and you open settings, the background should remain stage selection instead of changing to music box": the cards were drawn only on HOME and the pop-up, so ⚙ showed the music box behind its panel; they now stay behind it, veiled (the panel over them). (2) I/22 "at least one missing subject entrance not highlighted": the second soprano at b. 61 (B♭ A, then the subject's own G♭ F E♭ D♭ C at the first soprano's pitch of b. 1, and B♭, over the bass's dominant pedal, bb. 62-64): its fifth bent to a step and its ninth to a sixth, so no finder took it; added by hand (MORE_ENTRIES). Not taken: the tenor's B♭ A♭ G♭ F E♭ D♭ of bb. 38-39 (the same bent head, its own way at once, inside the second soprano's entry of b. 37). The cause found generally: the library's finder wants eight notes of the subject or more, its first note too, so a head stated alone (six or seven notes, then Bach's own way) or with its first note changed is missed; a pass over every fugue now takes the subject's second to seventh notes at its intervals and rhythm, the first note only by its direction, where the head holds a leap of a fourth or more (heads of steps alone, I/5 and II/6, are scales) and no entry is there: I/13 b. 11, I/24 b. 74, II/1 bb. 68 and 72, II/4 b. 51, II/16 b. 67 (II/15 b. 58, G major's broken chord in the coda, refused). Ledbetter (data/wtc/ledbetter-claims.txt) names for I/22 entries at bb. 32, 46, 55 and strettos at bb. 50 and 67, all highlighted; no published table of every entry could be verified here. (3) "the notes keep ringing in the player's voice even when they let it go. staccato has become impossible": in BLUETOOTH MODE the touched note scheduled at its written length now stops as the touch lets go (D306, WTC 1.30, which also keeps the voice from playing by itself). On the speaker, measured on the fake audio graph (a touch let go after 0.1 s), every sound of PRESETS, struck plain, joined, tied, detached or with the piano's envelope, is now 40 dB down within 120 ms: NOCTURNE 32-BIT's recordings were -20 dB then, SUPER 16-BIT's -32, SOLO VOICES' -39 (their own release, 27-53 ms a time constant); a player's note let go takes at most 25 ms (chip.ts LIFT_TAU). The subject's layers and an ornament's parts stop with the touch (a whole run, I/22 and I/2). A touch the system takes back (pointercancel; our handlers capture the pointer and the play area is touch-action: none, so a plain lift is a pointerup) rang on to its written end (D293); it now does only while another thumb is down (the overlap D293 was for), else it stops there. And AUTO, which turned BLUETOOTH MODE on whenever a stored delay was over 80 ms (a delay tapped once for earbuds kept it on the speaker for the visit): on by the latency the browser reports; a stored delay counts only for the output it was tapped with (the same 50 ms bucket of reported latency, or the next), and where the browser reports none (Safari) only in the page load it was tapped in. (4) "in score screen, there's no reason for the big gap in the bottom of the screen … quite bugged": the result sat in the music box's upper part, the play area under it empty; and its head took the sheet mode's class (.tb-sheet-head: absolute, white, full height), so in every display mode its title and lines were doubled over a white panel, the bars spilling out of it. The sheet mode's head is now .tb-sheet-headpaper; the result is an overlay of the whole screen, centred in the safe area, scrolling only if it must, on a tablet and a computer scaled up (its 7 px type was lost in a 1440 px window); and the cards were read before the best score was kept (a new best appeared only after a reload). (5) "add pause button to rounds": ❚❚ beside ✕ (Enter or the Pause key on a computer; Escape still quits, from the pause too); a hidden page pauses. Paused, the clock holds, every touch is let go unjudged, every sound and everything scheduled (the band's notes ahead, BLUETOOTH MODE's) stopped, no note goes by missed; RESUME clicks a bar of count-in and the music takes up again from the beat the pause fell in. Not with KIMIKO (her recording keeps the time) nor in a lesson. (6) "increase button area of both buttons, by occupying this area which currently is a useless border of the music box": the whole screen was already the bars' touch area (the play area lies over the box); what was drawn is widened: the box loses its 8 px side frame (its words keep their inset), so the bars meet its content, and the bars are lit to the screen's foot (a dark strip of 24 px was left under them; the beat's marks now over the bar). Portrait plays no bars (TURN THE PHONE SIDEWAYS). (7) "the cards of fugues ought to be updated with the inclusion of the player's highest grade and score": the best grade stamped in the picture's lower right corner, in its colour (S gold to D red), and BEST n (RECORDE, RÉCORD) under the sound; nothing for a piece not played. | play.html: BenchApp.tsx (LiteGrid behind ⚙, btLite, pause, result), Panels.tsx (className), bench.css; tools/touch/extract.ts (shortHeads, NOT_HEADS, MORE_ENTRIES), data/touch/pieces.json; chip.ts (LIFT_TAU); proto-game.ts (up: cancelled; pause, resume, countIn); headphones.ts (btAuto); sheet-view.ts (.tb-sheet-headpaper); paint-proto.ts (bars lit to the foot); LiteGrid.tsx (GRADE_INK, best, scores); i18n pt, es; test/d308.test.ts, d308-release.test.ts, d308-pause.test.ts, bench-lite.test.ts | WTC 1.32 |
| D309 | The owner approved the practice report's proposal (Articulation and dynamics in Bach's keyboard fugues, 26 October 2026, §6: sources, 84 ASAP piano performances of 29 WTC fugues measured): "implement per instrument etc.". **Articulation, per instrument** (practice.ts TABLES; the others only: the player plays their own): each note followed at once by the next in its voice held a hold fraction f of the time to that onset at the playing tempo, its gap clamp((1 - f) IOI, min, max) in ms (never over 0.6 of the IOI). Harpsichord (§6.2): running steps and thirds (IOI under 200 ms, or shorter than a quaver whatever the tempo: the corpus joins stepwise semiquavers in every voice, and at the game's tempi I/2's semiquavers are 238 ms, I/1's 326) joined 6 ms over; walking steps 0.94 (12-35 ms), slower 0.93 (25-70), thirds 0.88 (20-80), quick leaps 0.90 (0-25), leaps of a fourth or more 0.72 (40-130), the bass's 0.66 (45-150), octaves 0.55 (60-180), repeated notes 0.55/0.60/0.65 (45-200). Piano (§6.3): joined 12 ms over (consonant quick thirds 20), steps 0.93 (15-40), the bass's 0.86 (25-60), slow steps 0.86 (40-110), thirds 0.80, leaps 0.68 / bass 0.62, octaves 0.48 (70-200), repeats 0.45/0.50/0.60 (60-220). Organ (§6.4): quick steps joined (D302's crossfade, no overlap), steps 0.92 (25-60), pedal steps 0.88 (30-80), leaps 0.72, pedal leaps 0.62 and octaves 0.50, repeats 0.50 (60-250); ours where §6.4 is silent: thirds as steps, quick leaps 0.90, a manual octave 0.60. Every table: a suspension's or appoggiatura's dissonance joined to its resolution (6/15/0 ms); an upper voice's arrival at a cadence (fugue.ts cadences, every kind) before a new start 0.85 (40-120, piano 40-140); the bass's note reached by a leap of a fourth or more and going on by step f - 0.05 (at most 30 ms more). Precedence: the reading's marks (detached 0.75, staccato 0.5, slurs: D304), then suspensions (a tie is one note in the score), then the cadence, then the repeated note, then the interval. Suspensions found from the whole score (practice.ts suspensions): a note a second, fourth, tritone or seventh above the lowest other note sounding, on a beat (struck there, or held into it while another voice moves), resolving down a step to a consonance with the bass (I/1 15, I/22 39, II/9 54, II/22 49). The synthesized sounds by their voices' patches (practice.ts kindsOf, patchKind): a recording plucked, a synthesized pluck or a sustain at half its peak or under, a period envelope fading within 1.5 s: the harpsichord's table (CLASSIC CHIP's pulses, BLEEPS AND BLOOPS' vibraphone and marimba, SWITCHED-ON's bass); sustaining: the organ's (CLASSIC CHIP's triangle, GAME BOY, SID 64, TOMITA, the leads); a join on them stays D302's (no new attack, the 30 ms crossfade); only the sampled harpsichord and piano overlap by their tables. SWITCHED-ON's bass keeps D305's 0.8 (the owner's choice). VOCAL SYNTH keeps D305's SUNG. SOLO VOICES (§6.5, style "solo"): every step and leap joined, a repeated note on a new syllable (its consonant, no gap), a breath only at an upper voice's cadence arrival (f 0.85, 120-250 ms); no bass rule. HEAR THE SUBJECT plays the notes its reading leaves unmarked by the same table (readings.ts performSubject `rules`, from the sound chosen). **Character** (character.ts CHARACTER: one line a fugue, letter and reason, for the owner to correct; preludes, the Art of Fugue and the tutorial neutral): antico moves f of quavers and longer on steps and thirds +0.10, on leaps +0.20 (to 1.0: joined), lively -0.06 and -0.08 (the piano's upper leaps -0.13: §6.3's 0.55); octaves and repeats kept; the row's clamp scaled by (1 - f')/(1 - f). Antico (11): I/1, I/4, I/8, I/12, I/18, I/22, I/24, II/5, II/7, II/9, II/22; lively (24): I/3, I/5, I/7, I/9, I/10, I/11, I/15, I/19, I/20, I/21, II/1, II/3, II/4, II/6, II/10, II/11, II/12, II/13, II/15, II/16, II/19, II/20, II/21, II/24; neutral (13) the rest, I/2 among them (its readings already detach its quavers). **The piano's dynamics** (§6.6, practice.ts pianoLevels; PIANO (SAMPLED) only), from the whole score, never from which voice the player takes nor what they do (D293), never knowing the subject: at each onset with two voices or more the highest note sounding +1 dB, the lowest -1; contour against the voice's median pitch +1.2 dB an octave (top), +0.6 (inner), 0 (bass), within ±1; the beat +0.3, semiquaver off-beats -0.3; phrase arcs between the perfect and imperfect cadences (segments under two bars merged with the next) from -1.5 rising to +1.5 at 65% and falling to -0.5 at the arrival; a suspension's dissonance +0.6, its resolution -0.4; the last two bars rising to +1.5; summed and soft-limited (4 tanh(x/4)); layer 7 below -1.5 dB (sampled.ts below 0.8414), now brought up to layer 12's level (measured 3.0-5.0 dB under it, 4.1 on average: gain 1.41 to 1.6), so the layer is a colour and the level the model's. It replaces the piano's metric weight and arch (phrasePlain); the harpsichord and the organ are flat (level 1: D294 kept 30% of the swing on the harpsichord, the organ had the synthesized sounds' ±12%); the synthesized sounds keep theirs. The subject: over all 48 fugues on the piano its notes -0.11 dB against the others' in the same voice and octave (1.32: +0.02); per fugue at most +0.35 dB (II/16; no term knows the subject, the rest is the music's own), 19 of 48 above zero. All notes -2.6..+3.1 dB (1.32: -1.26..+1.65), 7% on layer 7. **Measured** (phrase(), the default playing, no reading; joined = joined pairs of adjacent ones; mean gap ms by context, negative an overlap; 1.32 -> 1.33): II/22 54-74 s (79 pairs): harpsichord 90% -> 85% joined, its leaps 87 -> 37 ms (stile antico), the bass's octave 87 -> 180, steps -31 -> -8 (overlap 6 ms); piano 90% -> 35%, steps -30 -> +2 (top) and -30 -> +20 (bass), leaps 70 -> 49; organ 90% -> 62% (bass steps +10 ms), chip 90% -> 57%. I/2 0-20 s (84 pairs, neutral): every instrument 77% -> 52% joined; leaps 59-73 -> 124-128 ms, steps (its semiquavers joined by value, its quavers apart) -31 -> +5 (harpsichord) / +10 (piano) / -11 (organ); thirds 57-95. I/1 0-25 s (95 pairs, antico): harpsichord 81% -> 79%, piano 81% -> 49% (quaver steps +4, bass +26), organ 81% -> 79%; leaps 78-98 -> 37-49 ms, repeats 287-326 -> 200-250. With the first reading (play.html's default), I/2 by Keller 63% -> 48% joined, II/22 by Bodky 80% -> 37-80%. The piano's level per bar (dB, max - min of its notes): II/22 2.4 mean, 3.1 at most (1.32: 2.4, 2.9), bar means spread 0.95 (0.72); I/2 2.3, 3.3 (1.9, 2.4), 1.45 (0.70); I/1 2.1, 3.1 (1.9, 2.6), 1.28 (0.45). Rendered (offline, chip.ts, integrated LUFS 1.32 -> 1.33): II/22 harpsichord -22.5 -> -22.6, piano -22.0 -> -21.3, organ -20.6 -> -21.2, chip -21.2 -> -21.2; I/2 -24.2 -> -24.4, -24.6 -> -24.3, -23.1 -> -23.7, -30.8 -> -30.7. Not done: section ends as arc boundaries (the game's sections overlap and cut mid-phrase), timing (agogic) accents beyond D294's, registration by character on the organ (one per fugue in the report: the presets are fixed), the arcs on the singers. Measured, not heard: whether the gaps the tables give at the game's slower tempi (I/2's semiquavers fall in the walking row only by IOI; they are joined here by value) sound natural; whether II/22's piano, now 37% joined against 80% (gaps 15-40 ms on its steps), sounds too articulated after D304. | practice.ts (TABLES, SHIFT, BREATH, decide, kindsOf, patchKind, suspensions, pianoLevels, DYN), character.ts (CHARACTER, characterOf), phrasing.ts (Style organ/solo, TOUCH without repeat/leap/levels, PhraseContext, phraseContext, phrase, marked, ART_HOLD), sampled.ts (SAMPLED.piano.soft), readings.ts (performSubject rules), HearSubject.tsx, proto-game.ts, engine.ts; test/d309-practice.test.ts; d294-sounds, d302-articulation, d305-sounds, bench-d191 updated | WTC 1.34 |
| D311 | EXPRESSION UNDER THE THUMBS (the owner's Decision 12, "one design per instrument", approved with four answers: the player's touch may shape loudness on instruments that have dynamics, in a small range, never tied to right or wrong, revising D293; the mapping as proposed; "do with each voice the optimal thing" for the others; no scoring, "not for now"). The bars' HEIGHT stays the line's (LADDER judging by direction, D296; slides glide to the next note); ACROSS is the player's: where a thumb lands across its bar shapes the note's attack, moving it sideways while it holds shapes the sustain. Across is normalised per bar, 0 the inner edge (by the box) to 1 the outer (the phone's edge), mirrored for the two bars (protos.ts barDynamic); one rule: toward the box softer, darker, more closed, toward the edge firmer, brighter, more open; the middle (0.5) the sound as it was; a sideways travel of half the bar's width the full sustain range (touch-expr.ts holdValue); every sustain change smoothed (setTargetAtTime, 60 ms). **Per preset** (touch-expr.ts TOUCH_KINDS, landing, holdMode; chip.ts Play.touch, Chip.landed, touchStage, touchVibrato, touchShape, Sounding.shape): PIANO (SAMPLED): soft to firm -4 to +4 dB (measured on the graph, net of layer 7's recorded -5 dB: 8.0 dB edge to edge), the softer recording (layer 7) under the middle, the firmer (layer 12) from it, its level making up the layer's own -2 dB so the loudness runs evenly; held: nothing (a struck note cannot swell). HARPSICHORD: no loudness; the inner three quarters the plain 8', the outer quarter the lute (buff) stop: a lowpass from 2 kHz at its start to 1.2 kHz at the edge and a short ring (a 0.22 s time constant after 30 ms: -15 dB or more 0.6 s on, the plain string under -3 dB); the only exception to the one rule (the owner placed it at the edge); held: nothing. Its loudness through the lowpass -0.3 dB (G3) to -2.4 dB (G5, at 1.2 kHz). PIPE ORGAN: under 0.35 the echo manual (D262's two keyboards): x0.6 and a lowpass at four times the note within 1.2-4 kHz (a flutier registration): -4.5 to -4.8 dB in all; that note alone; held: nothing (steady wind). VOCAL SYNTH: the vowel, closed toward the box ("a" toward "o", the front vowels toward "u": formants log-interpolated, e.g. the soprano's F1/F2 768/1104 Hz in the middle, 432/768 at the box), opened at the edge (F1 +12%, F2 +5%: 860/1159); held sideways a messa di voce of -3 to +3 dB; held still 0.5 s it blooms into a little vibrato (the voice's own, at most 16 cents; the bass, which has none, 10), straight again while the thumb moves (a move of 3% of the bar's width). SOLO VOICES: closed toward the box (a lowpass down to 3 kHz at the inner edge), open at the edge (a high shelf up to +3 dB of air); loudness -0.1 to +0.3 dB; held the same ±3 dB swell; straight tone, no vibrato (D305). CLASSIC CHIP and GAME BOY: the pulse width by thirds, 12.5% (thin), 25% (hollow), 50% (square) (the classic chip's own table: -2.3 dB at 12.5%, -0.8 at 50%; GAME BOY's loudness kept by the pulses' RMS); GAME BOY's wave channel the filter's brightness, the classic chip's triangle nothing at the landing; held: the vibrato's depth, the voice's own plus up to 16 cents outward, none inward (the classic chip 0 to 25 cents). SID 64: the width any between 12.5% and 50% (0.125 x 4^x, its sweep kept inside it, its loudness kept); the sawtooth and triangle voices the filter's brightness; held: the vibrato. MEGA FM: the FM index x0.62 to x1.62 (1.4 octaves edge to edge, the timbre throughout); held: the vibrato. SUPER 16-BIT, NOCTURNE 32-BIT, TRACKER (recorded consoles): the brightness (a lowpass to 4.6 kHz at the box, a +3.5 dB shelf at 2.5 kHz at the edge: loudness within -0.05 to +0.3 dB); held: the vibrato (a plucked voice nothing). SWITCHED-ON, TOMITA: the filter's cutoff x0.58 to x1.72 (1.56 octaves; loudness -0.6 to +0.3 dB); held sideways a wah: a resonant peak (Q 3) of up to 6 dB, its centre at four times the note swept 1.2 octaves either way (1.7 to 9.2 times the note, never on the fundamental), transparent at rest; measured on a sawtooth E2-E6, with and without the patch's lowpass: at most +1.2 dB (a first try, 8 dB about a fixed 1 kHz, reached +6.8 dB where the peak met the fundamental: rejected). BLEEPS AND BLOOPS: the mallet's hardness: the strike's FM burst and filter opening x0.5 to x2; held: the vibraphone's motor 2.4 to 12.6 Hz (5.5 x 2^±1.2), the combo organ, the tube and the bass the vibrato, the marimba nothing. **The others** (Decision 12's question 3, per instrument): none follow the player's shaping. The piano: a slow, small following was weighed and refused: a fugue's voices are equals, each its own inflection; a band rising and falling with the player's touch is a global volume knob, and players touch softer when unsure, so it would follow right and wrong by the back door (D293's complaint). The harpsichord has no touch to follow. The organ: the echo manual is the player's line alone (a trio's one hand on the second keyboard; the band stays on the main). Voices, chips, synths, BLEEPS: colour and vibrato are each line's own; doubling them in the band would blur the polyphony. The `feel` following moves only in the dynamics lesson, as before. **Right or wrong** never changes the sound (D293): the touch is the landing, the same for a wrong note; Play.level is not the touch's (D293's test holds). Expression earns no points (the same playing at the box's edge or the phone's scores the same). The dynamics lesson and a reading's level keep D179's full dynamic (no touch there, so nothing is doubled). **BLUETOOTH MODE**: the notes are scheduled 0.15 s ahead of their touch, so their attack takes the voice's latest landing (the first, before any touch, the middle); a right touch's sideways move shapes the note it scored (shape on its scheduled sounds). **Keys**: a MIDI key's velocity is the piano's touch (KeyPress.touch; other sounds the middle); the computer's keys the middle. **Shown**: a held thumb's glow a little stronger toward the edge (0.2 to 0.4), a small mark where it is across the bar, and a hairline back to where it landed once it has moved (paint-proto.ts). **Texts**: HOW TO PLAY (LADDER) "Touch either bar, anywhere along it, as the note reaches the line…" (it said at the note's height, D296) and one line on the across axis; THE LADDER lesson's first line; the mode's rules a line ("Across the bar: the note's colour."); pt, es. No sound.ts change (the families are in touch-expr.ts by preset id). | src/touch/touch-expr.ts (new), chip.ts (Play.touch, layer, lp, lute, pw, pwContinuous, fmScale, strike, open, hold; Sounding.shape; landed, touchStage, touchVibrato, touchShape; voiceNote, sampledNote, patchNote, singNote), src/bench/proto-game.ts (Held.land, landAt, touchFor, lastLand, KeyPress.touch, move, play, arm, heldSides), BenchApp.tsx (MIDI touch), paint-proto.ts, lite.ts (RULES), protos.ts, lessons.ts, i18n pt, es; test/d311-expression.test.ts | WTC 1.34 |

the "tremolo" was the level moving 0.56 dB at 1-3 Hz and 0.57 dB at 3-9 Hz (semi-amplitude; up to 1.12), from the 120 ms crossfades of the tiles (in the files: 0.44-0.59 dB at 3-9 Hz) and the seams, and the brightness 3.2% at 3-9 Hz. **Approaches measured** (the soprano's files, against D305's): (ii) granular overlap-add (100-200 ms Hann grains, 50% overlap, random places): 0.74 dB at 3-9 Hz, a 1.7 dB spread, the centroid flickering 14-17% (the grains' own brightness): rejected; (iii) the onset then a synthesized sustain of the singer's mean harmonic spectrum: steady (0.04 dB) but harmonics-to-noise 63 dB against the voice's 34 and its spectrum 12 dB from the singer's: an organ, not a voice: rejected; (i) kept, in two parts: **the files** (tools/playback/vocalset/build_sustain.py; VocalSet as before, CC BY 4.0, LICENSES updated): each note made of pieces of up to ten different takes of the one singer within 4 semitones (the alto's F3-G3 from C4, 5-7, as D305; a take the pitch shift spoilt, its subharmonic over -40 dB, left out: an E5 shifted to F5 had one at -13 dB), the same take never twice running, joined by 280 ms crossfades aligned to the period and held to constant power for the pieces' correlation, every take's spectrum held to the takes' mean (the vibrato's residue in the harmonics' levels gone), a slow aperiodic pitch wander of 4 cents (a pitch held exactly made every period-multiple lag correlate: self-similarity 0.47; with it 0.24), the level held within half a decibel; the same 96 files of 3.25 s (their 1.0-3.0 s still a seamless loop for the chorales), each a few bytes longer (an MP3 comment) so that a player's cache, keyed by length, fetches them anew; in the files 3-9 Hz level 0.44-0.59 → 0.08-0.18 dB, self-similarity 0.57-0.82 → 0.16-0.24. **The touch game** (chip.ts chainOn, sampled.ts Bake.chain, VOICE_CHAIN 0.45-3.0 s): a note longer than its recording is no longer looped but goes on with passages of it from places drawn anew each time (a hash of the note), crossfaded over 300 ms; a straight tone is nearly periodic, so two passages correlate by their phase and a plain sine-law crossfade swelled or sagged by up to ±3 dB (measured), so each passage starts where its waveform best matches the outgoing one (within one period; the best of six places by the least correlation through the crossfade, the recording's wander allowed for), the crossfade's gains are held to constant power for the correlation measured at each of its 11 points, and the first passage allows for the syllable's scoop or glide (scoopShift; 0.3 ms for the soprano's scoop, measured in Chromium, enough to cancel a D5); after 16 passages the last loops. Measured on the nine notes: no period (the 2.00 s peak gone; autocorrelation peaks 0.07-0.31 at unrelated lags, the larger at the 0.3 s floor: the messa's residue), seam dips -3.7 → -1.1 dB (worst -6.4 → -1.6), level 1-3 Hz 0.56 → 0.16 dB, 3-9 Hz 0.57 → 0.12 (every note within ±0.31 dB), 0.3-1 Hz 0.34 → 0.20, brightness 3-9 Hz 3.2 → 2.8%, 0.3-1 Hz 2.9 → 2.0%, self-similarity 0.61 → 0.35. Kept from D305: the real low bass (m8, D2 from F2-F#2), shifts within 4 semitones, the 0.15 s release (about 120 ms), the 1.5 dB messa di voce. Now: the player's sign twice the size on a dark backing with a tick to its note (grey once missed); over the bars "TR TRILL · SHAKE" (MORDENT, TURN); and until the player has shaken one ornament (kept on the device, `seen: ornament`; not in the tutorial, which teaches it itself) a line over the box as one comes, "A SIGN OVER A NOTE: TOUCH IT, THEN SHAKE YOUR THUMB", and on each bar the lesson's thumb shaking along the bar with UP AND DOWN (DOWN AND BACK for a mordent) · TOUCH IT, THEN SHAKE. (2) FREE ORNAMENTS (free-ornament.ts; ornament-play.ts freeOrnamentParts): on LADDER a held note struck right, alone, still due, without a written ornament, takes the player's own when the thumb SHAKES along the bar: two legs, down and back, a mordent (the step below, then the note); up and back a short trill (Bach's short Trillo, from above); a third leg a trill from the note above (the Explication) to the note's written end; realised at the tempo as the written ones (Bach's Explication, the neighbours as the bar reads them). A shake: each leg past the written shake's reach (0.4 of a row, at least 6 px), mostly along the bar (vertical travel at least 1.5 times the sideways), each turn within 300 ms of the leg before; D311's sideways expression, a slow drift, a slow back and forth, a rolling thumb's wander are not shakes (tested). Never changes the note's judging, earns and costs nothing (tested: the same score, notes and verdicts with and without): its sign rises from the note, faint gold. Not in BLUETOOTH MODE (the note sounds from the schedule) nor on the computer. (3) THE SUBJECT IN THE CANTUS STYLE (round three's direction B, the owner's choice): in the default box each entry wrapped in a soft halo of its colour along its notes, first to last (three soft layers, no blur filter), a bracket over it with SUBJECT at its left (its shape, STRETTO), the key and voice no longer in the tag (the colour says the voice); announced: halo at 55%, the bracket dashed, a breath mark before the first note, the last four beats a dot each (the beat under way emptying); an entry still beyond the right edge within four beats a tab "SUBJECT · 3"; sounding: the halo full, the bracket solid with its end post. Gold for the player's voice and once captured, grey once lost, as before; each note still strikes. The sheet modes and the interface templates untouched. (4) THE MARKS EXPLAINED: the dot is SHORT (staccato: STACCATO ✓ when the thumb lets go before three fifths of the note), the stroke APART, the slur JOINED, the wedge STRONG (ACCENT ✓ on the bar's strong side). | D318 | The owner: "I feel the game should slightly be more accepting of slurs, involving intersections of thumbs leading to a smooth movement between two notes with little new attack … I had a lot of errors when I actually felt like I was doing what I needed to get a slur"; "much easier to happen in a fast fugue (for example, f minor book ii)"; and a real run (II/18, soprano, 83 bpm, WTC 1.36: right 401/494, MISS 79, DOUBLE 30, STRAY 10, WRONG 14; the misses on the second and third quavers of the beat, the slurred pairs' second notes, "+0ms": no touch given to them). SIMULATED (test/slur-sim.ts: iPhone 13 landscape, 844 by 390, rows 8 px, the screen split down the middle as thumbs.ts does; a slurring player: the second thumb down 0–250 ms before the first lifts (20%: lifted 0–60 ms before), the first drifting 0–30 px toward the next note and 0–20 px sideways from up to 80 ms before the other lands, wiggling as it lifts (15%), iOS taking it back as the other lands (15%), the other landing on the first one's half (10%), near the inner edge (30%), placed from the held thumb, a quarter of the joined notes landed 20–120 ms early; I/2, I/1, I/22, II/22, II/12, II/6, II/15, 40 bars, three seeds, 4107 notes). Causes found, each alone: (1) EARLY LANDINGS IN FAST NOTES: D290 stops a note's window halfway to the note before (95 ms for II/12's semiquavers at 79) and took a contact within DOUBLE_MS (120 ms) of a strike for that strike doubled, so a second thumb landing early while the first held struck nothing (ignored, or a stray and a slip) and its note went by missed: 92.0% right (II/12 86%, II/15 81%, I/22 100%: tempo-bound), the II/18 report's pattern; a player rolling the second thumb in 40–160 ms after the first (15%) reproduces the report (II/18: right 418/494, missed 73, doubles 43, strays 35). (2) THUMBS CROSSING: a touch belonged to the half it landed in, so a second thumb landing on the first one's bar was judged as the first thumb, from its own touch: 86.9% right with half the slurs so. (3) iOS CANCELS: a touch taken back with no other thumb down (D308) ended its note with no join: legato 74% and a fresh attack on a quarter of the slurs. (4) THE FIRST THUMB'S DRIFT: reaching the next note's drawn height before the other landed, it slid onto it (101 slides of 4107: no legato, the chain broken) and the landing was ignored. (5) THE SOUND: a handover within the legato window was credited but struck afresh (a gap of 0–60 ms: every note a new attack). Not causes: free ornaments in the overlap (2 of 4107: the thumb giving way is already passive once the other lands), D311's sideways expression (sound only), the legato window (max(90 ms, 35%) holds the 0–60 ms gaps). Whole player: 96.3% right, legato 91.4%, a fresh attack on 26.1% of the slurs. FIXED (proto-game.ts; every one a THE LAB parameter, MECH, group SLURS): a touch landing on the half where the other thumb is down, the other half free, is the other thumb (thumbSide; not within SAME_THUMB_PX 24 px of the thumb down: that thumb rolling, D290's double, as before); while another thumb holds the voice's note before (or let it go within the legato window), a landing that is not its roll is for the next note (slurNext): within SLUR_EARLY_SHARE (half the time between the onsets, at most SLUR_EARLY_MAX_MS 250 ms) early it strikes it, earlier it waits, down, and strikes it on its time (once the holding thumb lets go, from the half-way point: no silence between), unless a touch of its own lands for it first or within DOUBLE_MS after (that one takes it over); two thumbs within SLUR_ROLL_MIN_MS (40 ms) are a chord pressed, no slur; a thumb landing on the note the other's drift just slid onto (within 120 ms) takes it over, joined, the sound going on (slideTaken); a touch taken back alone still ends its note for the join; a note joined within the legato window sounds joined (Play.legato: no fresh attack; plucked sounds pluck). In a passage "connect" counts as joined for the legato credit (LEGATO % counts it, the chain is kept as it stood, not lengthened) and earns nothing more (tested: the same score as "join"); the note's line "⌒ JOINED by the drag (connect: the chain kept, no bonus)"; D318's takeover of a drift's slide gives that credit way to the strike's. **(B) SENSOR** (a new tab; src/bench/lab/sensor.ts, SensorTab.tsx): the tap-strength test, with two probes side by side, after the lead's research: the CONTACT SIZE (WebKit on iPhone reports UIKit's touch radius as Touch.radiusX/Y and PointerEvent.width/height, twice it; Essl, Rohs and Kratz, NIME 2010, read four or more loudness levels from the contact area, the accelerometer poorer: about ±26% on equal taps, WebKit passing Core Motion's latest sample at 60 Hz) and the MOTION SENSOR's spike (the owner's ask). | D323 | The owner: "As for the possibility of integrating microphone as a breath controller, this is interesting and i want you to do research about it. If feasible, we may try out a prototype in the LAB (a blockflute or traverso, for example)." **Research** (scratchpad breath/index.html): feasible as a headphones mode. A phone mic blown on is the breath sensor Smule's Ocarina shipped (Wang, CMJ 2014: a one-pole leaky integrator on the mic amplitude, an articulator envelope; blowing "has significantly higher energy than speech"); Misra, Essl & Rohs (NIME 2008) a windowed-average amplitude above a threshold; Patel & Abowd (UIST 2007) wind on a mic is broadband and strong at low frequencies. Dedicated breath controllers (Yamaha BC3A analogue; TEControl BBC2 piezo, configurable curve and attack/decay; Akai EWI, CC2 by default) measure pressure, not sound: the mic gives loudness of turbulence, coarser and room-dependent, hence calibration. Safari honours only echoCancellation of the three processing constraints (MDN BCD 8.1.7: noiseSuppression and autoGainControl not supported in Safari iOS); with it on, iOS voice processing and WebKit bug 326286 (output crackles, iOS 26.2-27), off: play-and-record may route to the earpiece: headphones. The speaker beside the bottom mic leaks the instrument back: a blow-ness test and a measured leak are taken off, headphones still advised. Recorder: louder is sharper and its dynamic range narrow (players shade with fingerings); traverso: wider dynamics, the lips keep the pitch; tonguing (tu/du, Quantz's did'll double tonguing) is a stop of the air, so the breath envelope's dips are the articulations. **THE LAB's BREATH** (its own tab; src/bench/lab/breath*.ts): the thumbs finger, the breath sounds: no breath no sound; ROWS (FREE PLAY's rows: the row under the thumb, moving re-fingers) or THE VOICE'S NOTES (PASSAGE's bars, each landing fingers the next note, no clock); a new note while blowing glides in 6 ms (a slur, no attack); a TONGUE re-articulates (a chiff). Two synthesised voices (breath-voice.ts; public/samples/flute is a modern flute, 4.5 s one-shots): RECORDER near-sine, weak harmonics, breath noise round the pitch, a 25 ms chiff and a 25-cent start, 12 dB of range, brighter with pressure, the pitch ±20 cents over the pressure (a switch; the figure a guess, no measurement found); TRAVERSO darker, breathier when soft, 26 dB of range, no chiff to speak of, steady pitch. The mic (breath-mic.ts): getUserMedia {echoCancellation, noiseSuppression, autoGainControl: false, channelCount 1} in the chip's AudioContext, navigator.audioSession "play-and-record" while open (back to "playback"), an AudioWorklet (Blob, else data: URL) posting 256-sample blocks, else an AnalyserNode read each frame; opened once, kept. The DSP (breath-dsp.ts, pure): RMS of 64-sample chunks through a one-pole follower (rise 6 ms, fall 50 ms) and a fast twin (2/10 ms); blow-ness = spectral flatness (150 Hz-6 kHz) and periodicity (Boersma-normalised autocorrelation, 1-10 ms) on the pre-emphasised last 1024 samples, every 10.7 ms; the GATE opens when both envelopes are over the threshold and it is noisy (the lower of the latest and the smoothed) for 3 looks of 5.3 ms, closes when the fast envelope is under the threshold less 6 dB (hysteresis) or no longer noisy for 50 ms; the TONGUE a dip of the fast envelope 8 dB under the slow and back within 1.5 dB inside 140 ms, 60 ms apart; the sound's fade from the fast envelope (a tongue's stop and the breath's end heard at once); the LEAK (the voice's output RMS times a factor) taken off before all. CALIBRATION: breathe normally (8 s, 95th percentile = the floor), blow softly (4 s, the loud half's median), blow strongly (4 s, 90th percentile): opens halfway between floor and soft (at least floor + 6 dB), pressure 0..1 from there to strong; LEAK TEST (2.5 s silent while the voice plays); problems said ("blowing softly is hardly above breathing"). Every parameter a slider; settings on the device (tb-lab-breath-v1). Lines: "BREATH on · C5 · -24 dB (opens at -48) · blow-ness 1.00 (flatness 0.64, periodicity 0.11)", "TONGUE · C5 again · the breath dipped 16 dB and came back", "BREATH off after 1.68 s · 3 tongued", "♩ G5 · L thumb · slurred from C5"; the breath meter between the bars (envelope, raw level, open and close lines, open stretches shaded, tongue ticks, a column coloured by blow-ness). No mic: a message (Settings path). Measured: Chromium with a fake mic fed a synthetic 8 s take (two blows with 3 single and 9 double tongues, a hum as loud): over two loops 4 on, 4 off, 24 tongues, the hum never; 2.2% of a core in Node for the analysis. | D325 | The owner approved the musicians' panel's recommendations #1, #2 and #4 (Musicians' Panel Report, 27 October 2026, §§2, 9-11; the owner: "loudness is never tied to right/wrong"; "unconvinced by following authors' articulations … hard to track in play", so NONE the clean default). **(1) ARTICULATION BY INTENTION, NOT MAXIMAL LEGATO.** Found: LEGATO was the largest source of points (1,395 of 2,424 in a careful I/2 alto run; ×0.5 to ×2 a note along a chain), D289's legato window counted a hold of 0.8 as joined, and C. P. E. Bach's half-value touch (Versuch I.3 §22) lost about 60% of the final score. Now a join (a note following the one before with no rest) is judged by the hold of the note before: how long it sounded from its strike, as a share of the time to the next strike (1 when the thumbs overlap; a handover with a gap of 50 ms or less counts as joined, JOIN_HAIR_MS, narrower than D289's 90 ms / 35%, which on a quick note took a half-value touch for a join). The engine infers the player's touch, no menu: LEGATO from 0.9, ORDINARY from 0.62 (Marpurg), DETACHED below, per note value (a quarter beat or less, a half, a beat, longer), so "semiquavers slurred, quavers detached" is one consistent touch. A join is credited when its hold is within 0.15 of the median of the player's last six holds on notes of that value (the first two taken as they come). Where a reading is chosen and marks the join (⌒ or |), the mark is judged instead. The credit (ARTICULATION on the score sheet, which replaces LEGATO) is the same for every touch: ×0.25 of the note's points a consistent note in a row, at most ×1, and articulation's points are capped at 30% of the run's (MECH.artCap). HELD's bounds follow the inferred touch (held from 75% of the note for LEGATO, 60% ORDINARY, 45% DETACHED; SHORT under 50/40/30%). The legato counts stay as they were for the LEGATO lesson, the LEGATO CHAIN feat, the slur drawn and the lab. The score sheet's ARTICULATION line names the touch ("DETACHED TOUCH 99/100 · HELD 61/64 · RESTS 8/8"); the flash names it too ("ORDINARY ×3"). HOW TO PLAY: "Your touch is yours: joined (legato), ordinary or detached. ARTICULATION scores a touch kept even, not legato itself." (was "LEGATO scores more"); the tutorial's LADDER drawing "Gold overlap = LEGATO · detached is fine too: keep your touch even." (a) AGOGIC: a strike on a strong beat (a bar's first; its middle in a bar of four beats or more) or on a dissonance (a second, seventh or tritone against a voice sounding, a fourth over the lowest) up to 15% of a beat late is PERFECT (MECH.agogicShare), provided the next strike in that voice is back on the pulse (within PERFECT's 70 ms): else it is GOOD after all, its PERFECT half taken back. (c) BROADENING: in the run's last two bars a late strike is PERFECT up to 20% of a beat at the end, growing from nothing two bars before (MECH.broadenShare); the game's own final ritard as before. Scaled: PERFECT, the slide and waiting-note takeovers by the note's own time to its nearer struck neighbour; early and late windows and the split floor by the passage's median time between struck notes (set at START); the legato gap by the note's length; the slur's early reach the share of the gap alone (no ms cap); "last touch counts for" 3 beats, at least 1.5 s (II/18: 3.25 s for the game's 3 s). C · HAND-OVER: RELATIVE, slurNext, slurEarlyShare 0.15, thumbs together 0, direction from either thumb, double the same thumb's 40 ms, AT ITS TIME. Centres at the same place across meet on level lines (the black's height, exactly); a black and a white's outer centre meet on a slanted line, so each black key's tip tapers (full height to split − slant/2, half at the split, nothing at split + slant/2) and a touch there is decided by both across and along; a white key's two centres are the same key, so neighbouring whites always meet level. The black keys are drawn from the hit test itself (traced outlines). Sliders in the tab: the inner zone's width (default 60% of the bar), the black keys' height (0.58), the slant (the weighting: the taper over 20% of the bar; 0 = hard edges); pitch names on the keys optional (PIANO: a piano's names; IN THE KEY: the piece's spelling). (2) **WHITE KEYS: IN THE KEY (default) or PIANO.** IN THE KEY: the voice's seven commonest pitch classes in the encoded score (by notes), ties broken toward the key's harmonic minor / major scale, then the lower pitch class; PIANO: C major's. Over the 48 fugues (166 voices) the share of a voice's notes on white keys: PIANO mean 57%, min 24%, max 96% (per fugue, the voices' mean: F♯ major I/13 27%, G♯ minor I/18 34%; C major II/1 95%); IN THE KEY mean 88%, min 79%, max 97.5% (per fugue 80% (I/10) to 96% (I/5)); never less than PIANO. (3) **Across picks the key, so on the keyboard it shapes no attack** (neutral; D311's sideways sustain off too; PLAY WITH IT's sensor strength still works); slides follow the hit test (the distance moved both ways, white to black sideways included; the dwell as before), shakes as before (their reach on a semitone's mean height), DRAG: CONNECT glides to the key under the thumb. A landing in a note's window while the other thumb holds is a HAMMER-ON (B's strike, unchanged). A landing B would call a stray, while the other thumb holds the voice's note before (not that thumb rolling), from `prepShare` (1.0) of the way from the other's strike (or the note before's onset, whichever is earlier; 20 ms leeway, `PREP_FRAME_MS`, for the same moment) to its note, rests PREPARED on the next note: no sound, no strike, a faint dashed outline on its bar. It sounds only by a gesture: the other thumb's lift (a PULL-OFF, timed and judged at the lift, from the note's own early window on; a lift before that leaves it resting and silent, "the other thumb lifted … before its window"; an iOS cancel counts as the lift), or, behind `prepPress` (off), a PRESS of the resting thumb, read as a move down or inward of more than `pressMovePx` (4 px) from where it was `pressSettleMs` (60 ms) after landing: the other thumb still down, both sound until it lifts ("OVER-LEGATO 38 ms" as it lifts). Never at its own time: by the end of its late window it is MISSED (`prepLate` "miss"), or ("lift") the lift still sounds it whenever it comes, judged late. Hammer-ons and pull-offs sound slurred (joinedSound: no fresh attack, a string's slur); direction is judged from where the thumb rests, as B (its own last touch). WHAT HAPPENED: "… PREPARED B4 · L thumb rests 448 ms early while the other holds", "⌒ PULL-OFF B4 · the other thumb lifted: sounded now", the note's line "RIGHT · PERFECT · PULL-OFF" (or HAMMER-ON, PRESS), "… let go unsounded". **PRESS by contact size dropped**: the owner's SENSOR log (iOS 18.7 Safari, 146 taps) shows PointerEvent width/height in steps of ~24 px, soft and hard taps not separable by it (AUC 0.56), and almost never updating while a thumb rests still (8 size-only moves of 818; when flattening, only together with a move); the accelerometer separates them (AUC 0.98) but its peak comes 25–37 ms after touchdown. The game asks THE LAB as each such note is about to sound (ProtoGame.noteDyn; never a slide, never in BLUETOOTH MODE, never where a reading's level or the dynamics lesson sets it; the game never sets it): the note starts at the level known then (Play.touch and Play.level, pianoOnset), the strike read from the motion peak after the landing (Held.t0) corrects it over MECH.dynRampMs (rampTrim); a landing long enough ago (a thumb that waited for its note) has its strike read already and sounds at the whole level. MECH.dynWait stays FREE PLAY's. **Never judged or scored**: the sound only; tested by replaying runs (B+ with pull-offs and hammer-ons, B sloppy with wrong notes and strays) with and without it: the events and summary identical. **The STUDY never with it**: its own settings force DYNAMICS OFF, and a study take never gets the hook. Each note one line in WHAT HAPPENED as in FREE PLAY ("♩ B4 · pedal −6.0 dB · strike — (a pull-off: no strike: neutral) · total −6.0 dB · … · L thumb · PULL-OFF"; a struck note with its strike, peak, the references, the correction), also kept in the run's log (`dynamics`: the design, ADAPTIVE, the references at the start, the lines). **His third SENSOR log** (zone test, 52 taps): inner and outer alike (soft 0.23 against 0.21, hard 0.62 against 0.66; ratio ≈ 1), so **one calibration** replaces the per-zone one as the default (wtc.lab.dynCal: LEARN or LEARN BY ZONE, both zones' taps together; before any, D335's zones together; else 0.25/0.75); the ZONES report stays in SENSOR. His levels drift between sessions (hard median 0.95–1.18 before, 0.62–0.66 now, the phone turned the other way): **ADAPTIVE calibration, on by default** (dynamics.ts AdaptiveCal): the soft and hard references are the 20th and 80th percentiles of his last 60 strike peaks (PASSAGE and FREE PLAY), blended on a log scale with the stored calibration as if it were 12 strikes (moving from the 4th strike), never closer than ×2 (round their geometric mean); kept on the device at each run's end, so the next session starts from them; a LEARN starts it again from its own; off, the stored calibration alone; FORGET to the default. Shown in SENSOR (references now, where they started, ADAPTIVE, FORGET), in the run's first line in WHAT HAPPENED ("· DYNAMICS B · PEDAL + STRIKE · refs soft 0.21 · hard 0.64 (adaptive, 40 strikes)", FREE PLAY's too) and in the sensor log (`dynCal`). Some hard taps by the box read as soft (3 of 18): **the strike's curve trusts firm over soft** (strikeDb "firm", the default; D335's "even" kept): at or below the soft reference a cut of SOFT_CUT_DB (−2 dB in A and B; C, its span twice theirs, −4 dB) at most; from the midpoint between the references the full accent (+4 dB; C +8); linear between. Every total still within ±8 dB. The owner: **THE LAB's B+ · PREPARED becomes the game's base configuration.** B+ is B's values plus prepared notes: timing RELATIVE, slurNext off, the double contact the same thumb's only (doubleSameThumb), 40 ms (doubleMs), RUNS lenient, `prepare` on, `prepShare` 1, "Thumbs together" 0 (slurRollMinMs); everything else at its earlier default. Its rules: a landing is the strike, timed by the player; a thumb resting early on its note while the other holds is PREPARED and sounds when the other thumb lifts (a pull-off); nothing sounds by itself; fast stepwise runs are judged leniently from either thumb, the run's trend checked. **(1) Defaults.** MECH_DEFAULTS are B+'s, so play.html plays them (every mode: RELATIVE timing scales the windows on HIGHWAY, the preludes' lanes and the lessons too; slurNext, the double contact's thumb, RUNS and PREPARED act on LADDER only, as before). Every preset names WTC 1.51's values first and its own on top, so each keeps exactly what it was: B (B+ without preparing, its thumbs together 40 ms), C, D and E (their hand-over, no preparing); B+ equals the defaults. Audited and unchanged: STUDY (its preset B+, its file every parameter), TOOLS practice and BLUETOOTH MODE (the windows widened as before; the notes still armed on the schedule), the keyboard HIGHWAY and the EASY levels (the computer's lessons still pass played as taught), the tutorial's demonstrations (demoRun lands each note on its time: hammer-ons; every step still passes, under A too), HEAR THE SUBJECT (no judging), the preludes' rides (left out of RELATIVE's usual time between notes as before). **(2) Words** (Portuguese and Spanish too; Herr Bach within 20 words, no new step: JOINED teaches the pull-off and passes played either way, by pull-offs or by plain overlapping strikes): HOW TO PLAY (LADDER) gains "To join two notes: strike the next on time while the other thumb holds, or rest it on its note early and lift the other: the lift sounds it (a pull-off). Nothing sounds by itself." and "In fast runs either thumb may lead: keep the run moving the right way, and it is judged as a whole."; JOINED: "Joined: strike on time, the other thumb still down; or rest early on the note: lifting the other sounds it." / "The notes fell apart. | D338 | WTC 1.52. The owner's decisions on six points. **(1) WRITTEN ORNAMENTS UNSCORED.** An ornament written in the score (TRILL ✓, MORDENT ✓, TURN ✓) is still shown and still played with the shake (D278, D319), and still counted (shakes hit/of: THE ORNAMENT lesson's "You shook 3 of 5"), but earns and costs nothing, as D314's free ornaments: the 2 × multiplier bonus gone from ProtoGame.ornamentShake, so the score and the score sheet's totals hold none (there was no ornament line on the sheet to keep). HOW TO PLAY (lite.ts, pt, es): "TRILL ✓; done or not, it never scores or costs". **(2) THE READING'S ACCENT (>) BY TIME, NOT LOUDNESS** (src/bench/accent.ts). Bach's harpsichord and organ cannot strike one note louder; their players accent with time: the note before lifted a little early, a small silence of articulation, and the accented note leaned on, a hair late, and held its value (Couperin's *suspension* and *aspiration*, Pièces de clavecin and L'art de toucher le clavecin; the long, "good" note on the strong beat in C. P. E. Bach and Türk; the organists' articulation silence before a strong beat). ACCENT ✓ therefore asks three things: (a) struck no earlier than the PERFECT window and no later than MECH.accentLateBeats of a beat of the metre (0.15: an agogic lean, not a late note), or the PERFECT window where that is wider; (b) the note before, where the score joins it to the accent and it was struck right, let go at least MECH.accentGapMs (15 ms) before the strike (still down: joined; a rest or a missed note before leaves nothing to separate); (c) held at least MECH.accentHoldShare (0.85) of its written length from its written onset, judged at its end (riders, or a reading's dot on the same note, waive it). Loudness counts nowhere in judging: the old touch-strength test (d ≥ 0.6) is gone, and so are the reading's level checks (the subject at the reading's level ±0.2, the rest at its dynamics plan ±0.22), which judged loudness too; the reading's level and plan still shape the sound. The marks' legend (box, HEAR THE SUBJECT, PLAY pop-up, side notes): "ACCENT: LIFT THE NOTE BEFORE, LEAN IN A HAIR LATE, HOLD IT" (pt, es); THE READING lesson's goal "> ACCENT (LIFT BEFORE, LEAN IN)". The three parameters in the Lab's MECHANICS › READING ACCENTS. **(3) NO SCORE-SHAPED DEFAULT DYNAMICS FOR THE PLAYER'S OWN VOICE**: declined by the owner; nothing changed. **(4) NO PULL-OFF WITHOUT A RESTING THUMB**: declined; nothing changed. **(5) THE LAB'S RUSH READOUT** (src/bench/lab/rush.ts): after each run, beside timingLine's "on average N ms early ±S", the right notes' mean timing over the first, middle and last third of the passage by time: "early 15 → 38 → 72 ms (rushing)" (late …; mixed signs "thirds −30 → 0 → +20 ms"), RUSHING when the last third is at least 25 ms earlier than the first, DRAGGING when that much later; the RUNS list's line in short ("early 15→38→72 rushing"). **(6) THE COUNT-IN BY TIME** (fugue.ts countInBeats; ProtoGame's start and its RESUME after a pause, so play.html, the Lab, TOOLS practice and the study alike): the fewest whole beats of the metre (beatOf: minims in 2/2, dotted crotchets in 6/8 and 12/8), at least two, lasting at least 2.5 s, at most one bar and 4 s (two beats however slow); where a whole bar is under 2.5 s (a quick 3/8, 2/4, 4/4 at 120) whole bars instead, up to 4 s and never more than before (whole bars of at least four beats). Still laid on bar 1's beat grid before the first beat at or before the passage's start, so an upbeat falls between the beats as written; WAIT FOR ME keeps its one beat; touch.html's own game (no tempo given) keeps whole bars. I/2 at crotchet 60: 3 beats (3 s) instead of 4. | src/bench/accent.ts, src/bench/lab/rush.ts, fugue.ts countInBeats/countInStart, proto-game.ts (ornamentShake, play, judgeEnd, resume), mechanics.ts (accentLateBeats, accentGapMs, accentHoldShare), readings.ts markLegend, lessons.ts, lite.ts, i18n pt/es; test/d338.test.ts, test/d314-ornaments.test.ts, test/d308-pause.test.ts. |
| D339 | WTC 1.59. The owner: "final adjustments to our main instruments' sounds and response to player. Piano is a dynamic instrument and should allow for a WEALTH of possible articulations and dynamics … SLUR as opposed to LEGATO … aurally very clear … The playback … still a bit too mechanic … just make this sound like someone playing the piece. Harpsichord, organ … susceptible to the same comments" (the main four: PIANO (SAMPLED), HARPSICHORD (SAMPLED), the organ (chamberOrgan), CLASSIC CHIP). Phase 1. **Audit** (offline renders of the band's own path through chip.ts in Chromium, an OfflineAudioContext, every voice an "other", the first reading as play.html; I/2 bars 7-14, II/5 bars 5-12, I/22 bars 12-19, the four sounds): every onset within ±10 ms of the written grid (the band's offsets' SD 3.3-5.2 ms: the fixed jitter, the downbeat's agogic, the harpsichord's arpeggio), no time shaped by phrase, a cadence arriving exactly on the beat; the piano's levels a note's SD 0.85-0.96 dB in the passages (1.05-1.17 over the whole fugues), its level a gain only (the same colour loud or soft; layer 7 for 7% of the notes), its chords' top note 8 ms early; the tables' gaps the same each time (II/5 on the piano 118 of 139 notes "nonlegato", the same few ms each); the piano's release one exponential from the written end (12-32 ms), no damper residue, the strings above the dampers damped too; the player's lift capped at 25 ms whatever it ended (a staccato, a portato, a slur's end alike); a slurred pair's second note as loud as its first; the piano and the harpsichord in the shared room (0.8-1.3 s of white noise, no pre-delay, as bright at its end as at its start, 0.22-0.24 of the whole mix, the bass in it). **Measured against pianists** (ASAP's 84 performances of 29 WTC fugues, MAESTRO's Disklavier MIDI, measured here: Peter et al. 2023; Foscarin et al. 2020; Hawthorne et al. 2019): a note's velocity SD 11 units (median; quartiles 10-12), the bars' means 6.8, an onset's top note 8.4 over its bottom, a beat 1.2 over the notes between; the phrase-level timeline 60 ms from a smooth one (median; 46-116), local tempo SD 3.4%; an onset's notes spread 10 ms, the top note a median 1 ms ahead. **Now: the band's time shaped** (every sound; phrasing.ts RUBATO, rubato, Shape.stretch): every voice the same offset at a tick (a chord stays a chord), soft-limited to ±18 ms and with the per-note terms never past ±20 ms of the written beat (the player is judged against the written clock; BAND TIME untouched): within each phrase (practice.ts phraseBounds, the piano's arcs' bounds: perfect and imperfect cadences, two bars at least) 8 ms · (1 - 1.5 sin πx), a little ahead in the middle and behind at the ends (Todd 1985, 1992; KTH's phrase arch: Friberg, Bresin and Sundberg 2006); each cadence's arrival (not the last: the final ritard's) 13 ms late, reached over the beat before (the last notes lengthened: phrase-final lengthening, Repp 1992) and given back over a beat and a half; the first phrase eased in from the written time over two beats; a note let go before the next ends where the shaped time puts it (Shape.stretch; proto-game.ts and engine.ts add it). Measured: the band's offsets' SD 4.2-7.6 ms, at most 20, cadences 13-18 ms late. The jitter 2 → 4 ms (the piano 5), the piano's melody lead 8 → 3 ms (Repp 1996; Goebl 2001: the lead is the louder melody's by-product). **The tables' gaps varied** ±15% per note, fixed (ART_SPREAD, noteHash: the rule system's noise, no randomness from run to run). **The piano's dynamics wider** (practice.ts DYN): arcs -3 → +3 at 62% → -1 (were ±1.5), register ±1.25, contour 2.5/1.2/0.3 dB an octave within ±2, a melodic peak +0.5, the downbeat +0.5 (other beats +0.4, semiquaver off-beats -0.4), suspensions +0.9/-0.6, the last two bars +2, a fixed unevenness ±1.2 dB a note, the limit ±6; a note's SD 1.8-2.0 dB over the fugues (1.46-1.73 in the passages), bars' SD 1.4-1.7; at an assumed 0.3 dB a velocity unit the pianists' 3.3 and 2.0: we stay under them on purpose (a game's band; the subject no louder). **The subject never louder, enforced**: the wider terms let II/16's subject rise 0.5 dB over its neighbours by its own shape, so in each voice and octave the subject's notes are brought down to the others' mean where theirs is over it, never raised (the only term that knows the subject). **The piano's colour by its level** (Play.vel from joins(), the band's dB; the player's from Play.level; SampledInstrument.velTone): a high shelf at 1.8 kHz, 1.5 dB a dB, within -8..+5 (Salamander's layer 7 against layer 12 at C4, level matched: 8.6 dB darker at 2-4 kHz, 14.6 at 4-8 kHz for about 4 dB of level, measured here). **The piano's dampers** (SampledInstrument.damper): by register 45 ms (A1) to 16 ms (C6) a time constant, the felt's residue 2.5 times slower from three time constants on (Repp 1995, 1997: a tone decays audibly after the key's release); from F6 (MIDI 89) undamped (UNDAMPED_TAU 0.6 s); the player's lifts: under 0.16 s a staccato, crisp (≤ 20 ms: D308's 40 dB within 120 ms kept), a slur's end (Play.group ≥ 1, lifted alone) set down 1.25 times more slowly, a handover (a stop set ahead: the next note struck) the register's damper. **The player's slurs from the thumbs** (proto-game.ts SLUR_DB, groupPos; the sampled piano only, never a slide): a note struck while the note before in its track still sounds (or let go within D318's legato window) continues a legato group, else begins one; the group's first +1 dB, its second -2, later ones -0.5: a two-note slur strong-weak by 3 dB (and darker), as the eighteenth-century tutors teach it (Türk 1789, ch. 6; C. P. E. Bach 1753, ch. 3); no new gesture; loudness from the thumbs' timing, never from right or wrong. The player's voice (I/2's subject four ways, as the game sounds the thumbs; dips between notes against the note's peak): staccato -40.9 → -34.6 dB, portato -28.9 → -20.2, legato -7.6 → -8.4, slurred pairs inside -6.7 → -7.4 and between -36.9 → -30.2, the pair's second against its first +1.8 → -0.9 dB. **The music room** (chip.ts REVERBS.salon; sound.ts SALON_SEND 0.2, the bass three quarters): the piano and the harpsichord off the shared room (Preset.room 0) into their own: 18 ms pre-delay, RT60 1.4 s (on a phone 1.0 s of impulse), darkening, 180 Hz-6.5 kHz; no sustain pedal (the owner's, after Schiff); a note's own room send (Play.room) goes there when the shared room is off. | D340 | WTC 1.53. **TWO NEW TABS on play.html: MORE BACH and CHORALES** (the owner's brief; sources from the research report of 27 October 2026). **MORE BACH**, in five sections: ART OF FUGUE (Contrapunctus 1-11; 12 a 4 and 13 a 3, each rectus and inversus as two pieces; the four canons, today's BWV 1080/14-17; Contrapunctus 14, the unfinished fugue of D169, moved here from the bench's XIV tab, its data unchanged), MUSICAL OFFERING (the Ricercars a 3 and a 6), INVENTIONS (15), SINFONIAS (15), CONCERTO (BWV 1043, its three movements; the player takes Solo Violin I or II, `playable`, the others are the band). 54 new pieces from the Mutopia Project's LilyPond and MIDI (public domain; Inventions 1, 6, 9, 12 CC BY-SA 3.0, Jeff Covey), read by `tools/touch/more-bach.ts` (tools/wtc/lily.py through `lily_json.py`: the voices as typeset, spelled, the bars, each `\repeat volta` once as printed; the MIDI for Contrapunctus 1 and the mirror fugues, where the reader cannot take the voices apart; the two agree, 99.9-100% of attacks) into `data/touch/more-bach.json`. Computed as for the WTC: entries (the first two voices' openings, else the first imitation, upright, inverted, augmented or diminished: `imitation`; none in the canons and the concerto), cadences, keys (homeward, now in analysis.ts), difficulty (the cards' EASY/NORMAL/HARD from `difficultyOf`'s stars), character (character.ts, our reading), sound (piece-sound.ts: the harpsichord for the Art of Fugue, the Ricercar a 3, the Inventions and Sinfonias; the PIPE ORGAN for the Ricercar a 6; SUPER 16-BIT for the concerto, the only sound with bowed strings: its violin for the solos, its strings for the band, its bassoon for the continuo, in a six-voice layout added to it; no sampled string orchestra is offered since D288). These exceptions to D297 ("no piece opens on a period preset") are the owner's ask; the tests keep D297 for every other piece. Checked against second sources (`tools/touch/more-bach-check.ts`, privately: Sapp's kern for the Art of Fugue and the ricercars, the study's Humdrum for the Inventions and Sinfonias, CCARH's MuseData for BWV 1043): bars and voices agree (inversus and the Ricercar a 6 barred in half or double bars by Sapp), 98.4-100% of attacks (the Ricercar a 6 65.5%, the check's kern reader's fault at bar 9); the tables in each SOURCE.md. **Tempi** (`tools/tempi/more-bach.ts` → `data/tempi/more-bach.json`; tempi.ts `MORE_TEMPO`): no MusicBrainz sample yet, so an editorial reference (moderate, historically informed: the concerto ♩ 96, ♩. 40, ♩ 120) lowered as D312 lowers the WTC's (the busiest voice at most 5.5 notes a second, never under 75% of the reference): e.g. I ♩ 83, II ♩. 40, III ♩ 90. **CHORALES**: Bach's 370 four-part chorales (Sapp, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, as the chorale mode, C2), read from data/chorales/bach by `tools/touch/chorales.ts` into `data/touch/chorales.json`: any of the four voices (the soprano offered first), ties joined, as written (no repeats), at the chorale mode's ♩ = 60 (𝅗𝅥 = 40 in 3/2), on the PIPE ORGAN. **The fermatas**: each held one beat longer than written (the chord under it held, the music after it a beat later), and the band WAITS there (D328's BAND TIME, switched on for these pieces alone: `bandTime: "waits"`, wait points at the notes after each fermata, `fermatas`). **Credits** as the licences ask: each piece's pop-up (`credit`), each tab's SOURCES & CREDITS, the site's Credits; derived note data of the CC BY-SA and CC BY-NC-SA pieces shared under the same licences. **Licensing found** (the brief): the built site ships note data derived from Huron's WTC kern (pieces.json; fugues.json and preludes.json in the study's library), whose files reserve derivative electronic formats (open, as before);
