| D342 | WTC 1.54. **THE MANUAL, PHASE A** (the Two-Thumb Clavier-Büchlein, the approved tutorial design of 27 October 2026, sections 1–4, Parts 0, I, II): play.html's phone tutorial is now the manual's Parts 0–II, 14 lessons (0.1 HALTUNG, 0.2 PROBESPIEL, I.1–I.5, II.1–II.7), on material already in the game. **The owner's decisions on the design's open questions:** (1) Part I opens with Invention 1 (in the game), not a chorale; chorale material moves to later phases (chorales are being exported elsewhere) and Phase A depends on none: I.1's main material is Invention 1's right hand, bars 1–2 at 80% (the card's chorale and its II/9 fallback replaced), and I.5's checkpoint is Invention 1's right hand, bars 1–7 at crotchet 60, to the cadence in G (the passage ends on bar 7's downbeat G4). (2) Chorale fermatas: the band waits (D328's BAND TIME "waits"); recorded for later phases, nothing built in Phase A. (3) Unscored skills (graces, stops, the organ's repeat gap): no pass mark at all; such lessons pass by being played through (ManualLesson without `checks`; none in Phase A). (4) Each lesson sounds on the instrument that makes its rudiment audible, as the cards list (chip I.1, II.2, II.6; harpsichord I.2, II.1, II.4, II.7; piano I.3, II.5; organ I.4, I.5, II.3 with its play on the piano), never saved as the player's sound; the placement on the sampled piano. The other open questions stay open (the slide in an appendix, the appoggiatura gesture, VII.3's LAB exercise, inégale, the computer track's future, Contrapunctus XIV's last page, one take or best of two, German titles, Part VIII's sheet). **The lesson format, declared** (src/bench/manual/lessons.ts): each lesson its part, titles, minutes, sound, aim, HEAR (Herr Bach's demonstration of a stage, in one or more manners: plain, one-thumb, prepared, held; II.3 the existing detached/legato sound pair), its aids, its stages (DRILLs, then one PLAY, each a material: piece, voice, bars, tempo as % of tempi.ts or crotchets, WAIT, solo, EASY timing, sound), its lens (COUNTS, SHOWN, NEVER), its COUNTS as checks on the run's lens (figures and ✓/✗), Herr Bach's lines (at most two before, one after, D225), SOURCES. The PLAY passes the lesson (every COUNTS line met, the crowd never on); a DRILL is judged only by the lines it has something to measure and may always be left (GO ON); TRY AGAIN, WATCH AGAIN, SKIP FOR NOW after three tries (D289), THE LEFT HAND INSTEAD for II.7; a lesson begun once may go STRAIGHT TO PLAY; a keyboard player gets no introductions. **Drills** (src/bench/manual/drill.ts, design 4.3): a small text format (`drill "Name" key … meter … tempo … [wait]`, `voice` notes as pitch + w h q e s t [.] with checked bar lines, `band none | click [fade A B] | drone P | voice …`) compiled into a TouchFugue when the manual loads and registered with fugue.ts (registerPieces; deviation: at load time, not by a build step, so the text is the source with nothing generated to keep in step). Five drills (drills.ts): Schläge (I.1: one pitch sixteen times, the click fading out after eight: ProtoGame Options.click), Treppe (I.2), Pendel (II.1), Paare (II.4), Tonleiter (II.6), each held to the steps, leaps and repeats it claims. **The lens** (src/bench/manual/lens.ts, design 4.4) is an addition to RunResults only (`lens`, from the LabEvents ProtoGame already taps and the run's notes): struck, timing mean/spread/PERFECT, direction from a thumb's own touch, repeats right and the gap before them, doubles, HELD and RESTS, alternation (successive strikes under 400 ms or a written quaver apart), joins, pull-offs/hammer-ons/rests/let-go, RUN ✓ and run notes, leaps by thumb, D325's touch and consistency. The score is never touched (tested: the same run watched or not scores the same). Engine additions, none of them judging in the game: ProtoGame.lifts and heldNotes() (for the aids), Options.click and Options.demoStyle (the demonstrations' manners), and EASY "timing" honoured on LADDER (I.1 only: height not judged; easyOf still gives LADDER "direction", so no game run changes). **Visual aids** (src/bench/manual/aids.ts), each for its lesson only, quiet: the timing dial (I.1, the right bar's lower half), the move trace with a leap's needed move (I.2, II.2, II.5), the repeat band (I.3), the hold fill gold at three quarters (I.4), thumb letters L/R on the first take (II.1), the touch meter with DETACHED/ORDINARY/LEGATO zones over the box's foot (II.3), the prepared outline made clearer with PREPARED and PULL-OFF/HAMMER-ON tags (II.4), the run trend line green or red (II.6). Checked at iPhone 13 landscape with Playwright. **Gating and memory** (src/bench/manual/store.ts, `tb-tutorial-v2`): Parts 0 and I open; a part opens when the part before is passed (its checkpoint) or TESTED; a locked part held down offers ICH KANN DAS SCHON (its checkpoint once: passed, the part TESTED, its successors open); inside an open part every lesson is open, the next one marked; the game itself never locked. Carried over once from `tb-tutorial-v1`'s phone track (left in place: the computer's HIGHWAY lessons still live there): t-beat → I.1, t-ladder → I.2, t-hold → I.4, all three → I.3 too, t-thumbs → II.1, t-legato → II.3, t-leaps → II.5, t-ornament → V.1, t-subject → VI.1, t-reading → III.3, t-whole → II.7 and VIII.1 (its part passed); answered YES → every part open; t-dynamics (loudness) has none. D327's "tutorial behind the player" on a phone: every part open, or Part II passed or tested. ManualMap.tsx replaces TutorialSteps.tsx (removed); the TUTORIAL card reads THE MANUAL · PARTS 0–II, n OF 12 LESSONS. TUTORIAL_LESSONS stays in lessons.ts for the developer's bench (?dev=1) and its tests; the computer's track (LESSONS, TRACKS.computer) is untouched. **The placement** (src/bench/manual/placement.ts), offered to I PLAY ANOTHER INSTRUMENT and I PLAY A KEYBOARD (skippable: every part open): Invention 1 RH bars 1–3 (Part I: right ≥ 90%, |mean| ≤ 50 ms, spread ≤ 60 ms), II/18 soprano 1–5 (Part II but II.6: right ≥ 85%, turns ≥ 70%), II/12 soprano 1–6 (II.6 and I.3: RUN ✓ ≥ 2, right ≥ 80%), I/2 alto 1–6 NONE (Parts III and IV opened: consistency ≥ 70%, right ≥ 85%); one take each, then a second take of the worst offered; each take a lab log (LabRecorder, as the STUDY's) kept in memory; the game's MECH (B+), the sampled piano, no reading, no crowd; Herr Bach names the lesson to begin with. Not built (later phases): Compare (4.6), new demonstration manners beyond Parts I–II's, the exporters, Parts III–VIII (listed on the map as to come). | D345 | WTC 1.61. The owner: "iphone was jittering at a long concerto piece, by the end. why is this? too many samples loaded? any way to safeguard from that?" (BWV 1043, SUPER 16-BIT's six voices, D340). **Measured** (Playwright Chromium, iPhone 13 landscape, play.html unlocked, BWV 1043 III played through by `?auto=1`, 314 s, against Book I/4, 244 s): the decoded recordings are **not** it: 39.9 MB kept (97 one-channel buffers, all decoded when the pop-up opens, 165.6 MB decoded in passing), constant to the end; the JS heap flat (34.6 → 34.6 MB), the page's DOM and timers flat, the game's lists bounded (D210), ordinary play records no lab log (only THE MANUAL's takes, D342). What grows with the time played: (1) **the sound's graph where the browser's `ended` report fails**: the concerto makes 46,600 audio nodes (150 a second; Book I/4 13,000, 54 a second), and each note's are let go only on that report (D210's Bag); D211 saw the reports late or missing on the phone, and Safari keeps a wired branch in its audio thread's work (D210's hiss and slowdown). With the reports silenced the page left 46,412 nodes wired by the concerto's end in Chromium (36,752 on the fake context; Book I/4 13,367), growing all the way: the longest, densest piece suffers most, at its end. (2) **The frame's walks from the piece's first note**: the box's painter (the band's notes, the player's, the ornaments' signs, YOU, the reading's marks), ProtoGame.next and the rest after a held note (articulation.ts restAfter) walked every note gone by, every frame, and the ornaments' pass built a string for each of the band's 3,592 notes each frame: JS per frame 1.19–1.29 ms at the start, 1.40–1.52 ms by the end (script 82–87 ms a second). (3) **The room's level re-asked every beat** (`roomTo` from `groove`): an automation event a beat, 462 by the concerto's end, never let go in WebKit where the room idles (a period preset, `room: 0`). **Fixes** (sound, judging, mechanics and loudness unchanged): `Bag` notes each source's start and stop as asked (the browser's own calls) and `Bag.sweep` lets go any sound whose own schedule is over (its last stop, or a recording's end at its slowest rate) by SWEEP_GRACE_S = 1.5 s, reported or not; called at most twice a second from each note, each beat's groove and the game's frame; a reported end still lets go at once (never later than before, never earlier than the report: tested, none let go early over the concerto); a note that fails to build lets go what it built. The painter and the game begin from the first note that can still matter (src/touch/visible.ts: a binary search past the longest note and a screen's width, the list's time order checked once, else walked whole as before; the same notes drawn and the same answers, tested against the old walks); the entries' subject set made once per list. `roomTo` asks only when the level changes. **After**: JS per frame 0.80 → 0.84 ms (script 51 ms a second; Book I/4 0.67 → 0.70 ms, from 0.87 → 1.06), the room's automation 4 events, with the reports silenced at most 617 nodes wired, flat (Chromium; under 1,200 over a 15-minute run on the fake context); heap and frame intervals as before. Only an iPhone can confirm that its `ended` reports were what failed (Safari's Web Inspector: the audio graph's node count, or the timelines' JS and audio-thread load at a long piece's end) and that the jitter is gone; thermal throttling over a long dense piece is not ruled out. | `src/touch/chip.ts` (`Bag` deadline, `Bag.sweep`, `Bag.open`, `SWEEP_GRACE_S`, `SWEEP_EVERY_S`, `Chip.sweep`, `roomTo`), `src/touch/visible.ts`, `src/touch/MusicBox.tsx`, `src/bench/proto-game.ts` (`next`, the frame's sweep), `src/bench/articulation.ts` (`restAfter`); `test/d345-long-run.test.ts`, `test/fake-audio.ts` (`context`) | — |

| D347 | WTC 1.58. The owner: "in bwv 54, can you add the words to the music box? in some very discreet way, attached to the correct notes of course, and prolonged when the notes are prolonged (… 'TEUFEL', which when pronounced in bwv 54-3 is taken a long long run before finally ending in -FEL …). same for bwv 228." **The words read from the editions' MusicXML** (D341's reader, `tools/touch/musicxml.ts`): each `<lyric>` of the first verse (no edition has a second), its syllabic, text and extend, on the note it starts on; carried to its melisma's last note: the notes after it, in the same MusicXML voice, without words and without a rest between (a tie is one note already); a text of underscores only (BWV 228 writes "_" on four tied notes) is no syllable. Sung parts only: BWV 54/1 and 54/3 the alto (violins, violas, continuo, the piano reduction have none), BWV 228 all eight voices (choir II's few syllables in the fugue section too: its last six bars). **The spelling as the edition has it**, slips included (reported, not corrected): 54/3 "An-dahct" (b. 27), "An-dacjt" (b. 44), "g;eich" (b. 47), "aug-ge-bracht" (b. 64), "wi-de-stan-den" (b. 44), "schö-den" (b. 25) and "schön-den" (b. 39) for Bach's "schnöden", "wie-der-stan-den" beside "wi-der-", "da ge-macht" missing "von" (b. 49); 54/1 "tifft" for "trifft" (b. 44); BWV 228 "dei Gott" (Bass I), "den ich" (Tenor II), "bei-dei-nam" (Bass I), "rech-ten Hand" and "durch die Hand te Hand" (Alto II), "Ge-rech rech-tig-keit" (Soprano I), "mei-ner-Ge-rech-tig-keit" (the edition joins the two words in several parts), choir II's fragments in the second section ("set.", "denn ich set, er-lö set", "ich ha-be ru-fen"); "thut", "Laß" are the period's spellings. **Hyphens**: after a syllable written begin or middle (so "Teu-" before "fel,", which the edition marks single). BWV 228's edition (from Capella) loses the hyphen after a syllable sung to a melisma ("wei" "che" both single; "er-hal" begin and end, then "te" single): a syllable written single or end, not ending in punctuation, is joined to the next where the same two are written joined elsewhere in the score more often than written apart (`hyphensOf`; 257 hyphens so, e.g. wei-che ×62, lö-set ×39, hal-te ×25; a slip joining "bei-dei-nem" once is outvoted); the chorale's words, which no other part sings, joined by a short list in more-bach.ts (al-ler, Freu-den, Le-ben, ge-ge-ben, fas-se, Her-zen, hin-ge-lan-gen, um-fan-gen). **Stored** in `data/touch/more-bach.json` (the MORE BACH chunk loaded on demand, D343; `lyrics`: per voice, three values a syllable: onset after the last, length to the melisma's end, text with a trailing "-" where a hyphen follows; +52 kB, +4 kB gzipped; no other piece has the field). **Checked by hand** against the editions: BWV 54/3's "Teu-" from bar 10's last crotchet over 40 notes to "-fel," in bar 13 (again bars 16-17, 8 notes; 54-57, 40; 60-63, 27); BWV 228: Soprano I, Bass I, Soprano II at bar 1 ("Fürch-te dich nicht"), Soprano I's "wei-che" over two quavers (b. 10), the chorale's "Brunn al-ler Freu-den" (b. 84-85), the fugue's alto "er-lö-set" ("lö" over 19 notes, bars 81-83). **Drawn very discreetly** in the CLASSIC box (MusicBox.tsx `paintLyrics`, `src/touch/lyrics.ts`): the player's voice only (the others nothing: eight voices would be clutter), the box's 3×5 pixel font (upper case, umlauts as its marks) at 55% opacity in the box's light ink, each syllable at its first note's start just under that block, lower where the word passes under a lower block of the player's (drawn before the blocks, which stay on top), a line lower (two at most) where it would touch the words before; a hyphen midway between a word's syllables, or just before the next after a melisma; a melisma's line, faint (45%), from the word to its first note's end, then a pixel under each of its notes to the last ("TEU ___ … -FEL,"). Twice the size where the rows are 16 canvas pixels or more (it scales with the box); each word drawn once into a small canvas kept for it; only the syllables in view (binary search) and their notes looked at. YOU (D279), where the words stand under the notes, is drawn over the voice's next entry instead (after a rest or a long note), not under it. The pixel font gains ";" and "?"; `pixRaw` draws text untranslated. Pieces without words unchanged. Found in passing (D341's import, unchanged): BWV 54/1's alto staff writes a second voice in bar 26 (small notes with the words, the plain two quavers below), both in the game's notes. Cadences and keys as for the WTC, no entries (as BWV 1043). **Checked** (`tools/touch/concertos-check.ts DIR`, privately): independent editions agree on 91-99.6% of attacks (1052 Arpicembalo and Kopecny, 1055 Mutopia, 1057 Marcialis), the solo's right hand on 92-100% in the quick movements, 84% in 1052's Adagio (ornaments written out differently); 1062's strings against BWV 1043 a tone lower 85-98%, its harpsichords' top lines against the solo violins 90-98%; 1056, 1058, 1063, 1064 rest on one source. **Tempi** (tools/tempi/more-bach.ts; references in concertos.ts, period bands' pace a little under): lowered so the busiest line offered keeps to 5.5 notes a second, no floor (e.g. 1052/I ♩ 100 → 82, 1054/III ♪ 240 → 110, 1065/II ♩ 46 → 41); a line that would hold a movement under 40% of its reference is not offered (none now). **Sound:** SUPER 16-BIT (as BWV 1043): a layout for each voice count (7; 9 for 1057; 10 doubles; 11 triples; 13 for 1065): its harpsichord for the hands (×0.8, the inner notes lighter), the harpsichords panned apart, its strings for violins and viola, its bassoon for the continuo (the last voice: a phone's bass), its flute for the recorders; a harpsichord line never louder than a string line (tested). **Load:** a chunk of their own (`src/touch/concertos-data.ts`, 4.05 MB, 842 kB gzip) fetched with the MORE BACH notes (fugue.ts loadExtras); the index (extras-index.json) lists them; tsc reads the 4 MB file's type from `data/touch/concertos.d.json.ts` (tsconfig `allowArbitraryExtensions`: inferring it ran out of memory). | D349 | WTC 1.65. The owner on PIPE ORGAN: "The organ sounds terrible though and needs some improvement. It is sounding too much like four different instruments instead of a single organ with different stops being used by a professional organist in a performance." **Diagnosis** (each voice rendered alone through the game's engine, I/2 and II/9): D304 had registered the organ as a trio of colours on purpose, so the four instruments were by design. Each voice had its own stop (a flute, a Krummhorn, a Principal, a Trompete) over a recorded reed pedal, and the brightness followed the voice instead of the register (spectral centroid, I/2 soprano/alto/bass 603/1724/933 Hz; II/9 S/A/T/B 499/742/1213/886 Hz). The voices were also panned apart (±0.25) and each had its own envelope (attack 10-35 ms, release 70-120 ms), its own slowly drifting tuning (`drift`) and its own send (0.10-0.14) into a church whose send was high-passed at 250 Hz, which left the pedal's fundamentals dry, as if in another room. The player's note was 1 dB louder, 2.5 dB brighter and released longer (MINE_LEVEL, MINE_TONE, leadOf). Each band note had its own ±6 ms timing scatter (phrasePlain) on top of the full phrase rubato. A joined note had no new attack, which is how a wind player joins notes, not an organist. **One organ** (`sound.ts`, `registration.ts`, `chip.ts`, `phrasing.ts`, `touch-expr.ts`, `sampled.ts`). (1) Every manual voice plays one patch, the default `orgPrincipal`: Principal 8′ with its Octave 4′ and a soft 2′. The pedal (`orgPedal`) is a related division: Subbass 16′, Octavbass 8′, Choralbass 4′, with a 2′ added on a phone (`Patch.pipe.phoneOsc`). Through a phone-speaker approximation the pedal measures +0.2 dB against the manuals (−0.7 before, −2.1 without the 2′). The voices are placed within ±0.05; each note goes ±0.12 by its key (`Patch.pipe.case`: the case's C and C♯ sides). Each key's pipe sits a fixed fraction of a cent from true (`pipe.cents`), whoever plays it, and the drift is gone. All manual patches share one release (60 ms), one level (0.3; loudness matched through the ranks' gains, measured within about 1.5 dB) and one send (`ORGAN_SEND` 0.4) into one church. The church (`REVERBS.cathedral`) has RT60 2.6 s on a computer and 1.7 s on a phone (`Reverb.phoneRt60`), a 30 ms pre-delay and the send high-passed at 110 Hz; Boren 2021 simulates the occupied 1723 Thomaskirche at 2.3 s at 1 kHz, as long at 125 Hz. A joined note's pipe speaks anew, with its own attack and chiff (`Chip.piped`); the organist's legato is in the release. The player's note is the band's pipe exactly (no MINE lift, `leadOf` unchanged). (2) **Registration as an organist's** (D322 kept: same buttons, same semantics). One stop draws its own patch: FLUTE `orgFlute` (Gedackt 8′, Rohrflöte 4′), PRINCIPAL, REED `orgTrompete`. MIXTURE without the reed is the plenum: the VCSL organ's recorded plenum (`orgPleno`, a new set `plenoOrg` from the pipeOrgan folder, loaded with the preset through the new `Preset.also`). Other combinations become one note of their ranks, as before. Without TUTTI the stops apply to the player's voice alone, as a second manual (a reed cantus firmus, the flutes) at the same level. With TUTTI the whole organ takes them, and the pedal follows the manual (`pedalPatch`): the soft pedal `orgPedalSoft` under the flutes alone, under MIXTURE the plenum's recorded pedal `orgPedalPleno` (D304's "cool" bass). The pedal's 16′ is drawn by default; the player's 16′ button pushes it in (`orgPedal8`; the separate Subbass note and `SUBBASS`/`SUB16_LEVEL` are withdrawn). The Krummhorn is withdrawn. (3) ORGANO PLENO (the zone, D181/D186) draws the mixture on the whole organ until the zone ends (`crowned`); the old `Play.pleno` doubling stays unused. (4) The echo manual (D311) now plays the soft flutes on a second manual (`Play.echo`; ECHO_GAIN and the lowpass kept) instead of dulling the same stop. The pedal has none. (5) The band articulates by release: on the organ a manual's notes are struck together (no per-note scatter), with half the phrase rubato (`ORGAN_ENSEMBLE.rubato` 0.5, within ±9 ms) and the table's gaps varied ±5% instead of ±15% (`ORGAN_ENSEMBLE.spread`). There were no dynamics on the organ to remove: its level stays 1 (D309). An A/B page (local, scratchpad) covers five passages, four registrations, the player on the soprano, the bass with and without 16′, a reed cantus firmus and the echo manual, each also through a phone-speaker approximation. **Sources checked:** Boren, "Acoustic simulation of J.S. Bach's Thomaskirche in 1723 and 1539", *Acta Acustica* 5, 14 (2021); BYU OrganTutor, "Articulation" (before about 1750 legato is not the basis; the line is shaped by the release); Acoustics Today, Spring 2017, p. 14 (flutes speak fastest; principals have a strong second partial in the attack). **Open for the owner's ears:** the default (Principal 8′ 4′ or the recorded plenum); the church's wetness (9 dB more tail); the pedal's 16′ by default; the player's voice now told apart only by colour in the box and by the stops; the C/C♯ placement. `test/d349-organ.test.ts`; D297, D302, D311 and D322 tests rewritten for one organ. |

| D352 | WTC 1.62. The owner: "subject highlights are still not up to my taste. i would like the color of the notes to change a bit when the subject is playing, consistently to some hue variation of primary color; in double fugues and triple etc, the color will be distinct. it is still not obvious whether some voice is playing the subject when i'm trying to score well … this includes when the subject is playing in the non-player voices too … always less pronounced than the player's voice … when a subject is playing in a distinct non-player voice, it might even get as protagonistic as the player's voice." (After D186's halo and tag, D269's other voices thin and flat, D287's gold frame removed, D320's shaded band and embers.) **The subject's colour** (CLASSIC box, the DEFAULT display; `src/touch/subject-hue.ts`): each subject a primary, the same in every piece: the first RED, the second BLUE, the third YELLOW (the analysis's `theme`: I/4, II/4, II/14, II/18, II/23, Contrapunctus XIV; answers, inversions, augmentations and diminutions are their subject's; the countersubject none). A subject note, in any voice, the player's too, is drawn in its voice's colour bent to the primary: the primary's hue leaning a little toward the voice's own (30% of their distance, at most 12° for red, 20° for blue, 6° for yellow: more and a green voice's red is the alto's orange, yellow's turns orange), saturated, the voice's lightness met halfway by the subject's (yellow light, red and blue darker); a keel of the voice's own ink along the block's foot keeps whose it is (with the legend and the voice's register). **The second cue** (not hue): a rim of the primary round each subject note of the others (40% alpha, 85% while raised); **the protagonist**: while an entry sounds its voice is raised, from 150 ms before its first note to 150 ms after its end (smoothstep): the others' subject notes from half a row to 72% of a row, 75 → 92% opaque, the rim strong, a faint halo of the primary; the player's own entry gets a halo of the primary round its white outline. Never the player's look: no white outline, no voxel shadow, never a full row for the others, so the player's line stays the one outlined. Before an entry its notes are already tinted (thin): it can be seen coming. **Quieted or removed**: the shaded band 15% → 9% and its hairlines gone, in the subject's primary for every voice (it was gold for the player's and once captured, the voice's ink for the others; grey once lost kept); the SUBJECT / SUBJECT 2 · INVERSION / STRETTO word in the primary; the embers in the primary (gold for the player's before), 6 a note from the others' (10 from the player's); the per-note "voice:onset" strings built every frame for the others (D345's set) gone. Unchanged: YOU, the words (D347), the ornaments' signs, the reading's marks, the bosses, the cadences, the SHEET displays and the other interfaces (their own subject marks), the sound and the score. **Membership** made once per list of notes and entries (an Int16Array of the entry each note belongs to, by voice and onset, the first entry claiming a note keeping it; a WeakMap, made again only when the list grows): the frame looks up an index and a kept colour, allocates nothing. Tested equal to the ear's subject notes (subject-fx.ts SubjectCues) and their theme in every fugue. **Colour-blind**: a Machado deuteranopia simulation of the I/4 screenshot: blue stays blue, yellow bright yellow, red a dull khaki (told from yellow by lightness), and the raise and the rim are shape and brightness, not hue. Screenshots before/after (iPhone 13 landscape, play.html `?auto=1`, TOOLS practice from the bar): I/2 (alto; the soprano's answer, bar 3), I/4 (tenor; bars 50-56, all three subjects), I/1 (alto; the stretto at bar 7), Contrapunctus XIV (alto; bars 234-239, the three subjects together), in the session's scratchpad. Only the phone can say whether the raise is too strong against the player's line. | `src/touch/subject-hue.ts` (`SUBJECT_HUES`, `SUBJECT_RIMS`, `subjectInk`, `subjectInkOf`, `raiseOf`, `RAISE_FADE_MS`, `membership`, `themeOf`), `src/touch/MusicBox.tsx` (`paintClassic`), `src/touch/box-fx.ts` (`ribbons`, `strikes`); `test/d352-subject-hue.test.ts` | — |
| D353 | WTC 1.66. **THE ORNAMENTS, PHASE 0** (the design report "Die Manieren for two thumbs", 28 October 2026; the owner's answers). **(1) MORE BACH's signs found.** ornamentsOf looked up the Inventions and Sinfonias as bwv772… while ornaments.json keys them inven01–15 and sinfo01–15: they played with none of their 310 signs. ornament-play.ts ORNAMENT_ALIAS maps bwv772–786 and bwv787–801; all 310 land on their notes (tested), Invention 1's mordents among them. **(2) THE CONTOUR READER** (the owner's design: "a mordent is a quick tap-down-up, a trill an even quicker tap-down-up-down-up; the movement may be very small; its speed relative to the tempo"), free-ornament.ts ShakeReader, replacing D314's free reader and D278's written one: a leg is a run one way along the bar between turning points, ending at a reversal by the reach or at rest (no progress for 50 ms, read on the game's frame clock: a still thumb sends nothing); it counts when it travelled the reach, max(3 px, 0.15 row) (D314: max(6 px, 0.4 row)), is steep over the WHOLE leg (along ≥ 1.5 × across, across floored at 1.5 px; D314 judged it where the reach was passed) and is quick: each leg within Lm = clamp(0.30 beat, 90, 220) ms, a trill's legs on average within Lt = clamp(0.20 beat, 70, 160) ms, the beat the metre's at the tempo played; a slower leg is dropped. Two legs, down first: the MORDENT; up first: the PRALLTRILLER on a note reached by a falling second and joined to it (Versuch I.2.3 §§31, 34; realised N U N at twice the ornament speed, no faster than the beat's 32nds), the SCHNELLER on a note under half a beat with a lower note after (I.2.8; its last note detached), else Bach's short Trillo; a blurred first leg (under a third of the second): the Pralltriller after a falling second, else the mordent. The owner's answer 4: a clear direction wins even where unstylish; the music only names an ambiguous gesture and the up-down form. A third leg of at least half the first two's size: the TRILL, either way first, realised from the UPPER note (the owner's answer 1, both Bachs) from the moment it is read, lasting while the legs come (D319 kept); with no room for four trill notes and the Nachschlag, the TURN (I.2.4 §9). Two legs begun later than S = clamp(0.6 beat, 180, 500) ms after the landing, past 40% of a note of a beat or more and 600 ms, not before a falling second: the TURN AFTER THE NOTE (I.2.4 §§21–22). The long mordent is no gesture (answer 6; a written M on a long note still realised as written). The S-turn, the loop and the flick stay for Phase 1 (the Lab). Written signs: the same reader, either way first (D289), the sign deciding what sounds; the notes begin once a quick leg has turned back (or stopped after D289's 0.4 row of travel: a settling roll of a few px is not the ornament); done at the legs needed (a trill 4; mordent, short trill, turn 2: their notes are realised now, not one a move), the last leg also counted at the lift. **(3) THE TRILL'S ENDING** (the owner asked which sounds more musical; decided here): C. P. E. Bach's rule (I.2.3 §§13, 16): the Nachschlag (… U N L N, beaten to the end) on a note of a beat or more, after a leap, or on the note before a cadence's arrival; none in a falling series or on a short note (room for fewer than four trill notes and the Nachschlag); else the trill settles on the note (the Explication's). The band's trills and the player's; the player's trill stopped early closes (N) L N where the rule gives one. MECH › ORNAMENTS › TRILL ENDING: RULE (default) / SETTLE (D319's). **(4) THE OVERLAY** (answers 3): ProtoGame.performed ("track:note" → what it became, whose, its notes as sounded; a trill's grow note by note, the settled main note to the written end) and bandPerformed (the band's written ornaments as scheduled), read by MusicBox (paintOverlay; ProtoGame.overlay()) and by the score sheet's count (D327: freeTally of the free ones, now naming Pralltriller, Schneller and turns, en/pt/es). Each realised note on the box's rows (the neighbour's staff step, D332's lift against the key), only once it sounds, 60% of the block's height at 60% opacity in gold (the player's), the band's 30% high at 30% in their ink; notes narrower than 3 px drawn as a 1 px zig-zag between the rows; fading over a second once the note has gone by (OVERLAY_FADE_S). **(5) THE GUARDS.** No free reading on a run's notes (but its last), on a note riders follow, on chords, sweeps or traced lines, or on a note shorter than two 50 ms legs and two ornament notes. After an ornament read, the thumb's D286 reference returns to its landing at the lift when it ended within a row of it. THE SLIDE GUARD: on a phone's LADDER a slide that reached its note fires only once the thumb has not turned back by the reach for MECH.slideConfirmMs (60) and no leg is under way faster than reach / Lm; judged at the moment and place it reached the note. A resting PREPARED thumb's moves go to the reader too; once a shake is read on it, PRESS is disabled for it. BLUETOOTH MODE and the computer's keys unchanged (no reading; the written ornament plays itself). MECH.ornGestures off: no reading at all. Ornaments stay unscored (D338). **MECH › ORNAMENTS** (all in MECH_SPEC for the Lab): ornGestures, freeOrnaments, shakeReachRows 0.15, shakeReachMinPx 3 (the owner's answer 5), shakeSteep, shakeWholeLeg, ornLeg{Beats 0.3, MinMs 90, MaxMs 220}, trillLeg{Beats 0.2, MinMs 70, MaxMs 160}, ornStart{Beats 0.6, MinMs 180, MaxMs 500}, turnAfterShare 0.4, turnAfterMinMs 600, trillEnding, slideConfirmMs; shakeWindowMs removed. The Lab's FREE PLAY reads with the same reader (its tempo's beat). **Tested** (test/d353-ornaments.test.ts, the owner's 27 October logs as fixtures, data only, test/fixtures/owner-2026-10-27/): the reader over all 2,093 held touches reads exactly the report's 20 deliberate ornaments (I/12's trill in nine takes, II/12's mordent in nine, II/23 take 4, II/18 take 4) and nothing else; the 107 runs replayed: with the reading on or off every note's verdict, join and time identical; with the slide guard every note right, wrong or missed as before, one join changed (II/24: a left thumb drifting 17 px onto the next note and back, a slide 79 ms early, no longer slides; the right thumb's strike takes the note, PERFECT); II/12's written mordent now read in 9 of 10 takes. HOW TO PLAY (INTERMEDIATE, ADVANCED) and THE ORNAMENT lesson describe the gestures (en, pt, es). On screen with `&auto=1` the voice shakes its written ornaments (screenshots). |

| D355 | WTC 1.70. **COLOUR PLANS and each fugue's own sound** (the owner: "if player is using harpsichord, some fugue will have moments where it's common to switch manuals … the playback voices could do the switch independently of what player does … each preset might have a chance of having those tailored things in specific fugues … every fugue needs a default sound chosen, tailored to it"). **1. The plans** (`src/touch/colour.ts`; written with a reason for each moment in `tools/touch/colour-plans/`, packed by `tools/touch/colour-plans.ts` into `data/touch/colour-plans.json`, a chunk loaded on demand through `colour-data.ts`): for a piece and a sound, moments by bar (a fraction of a bar where the music asks: Bodky's 61⅝) where the BAND changes colour as a player of that instrument would: the harpsichord's manuals and stops (8′, 8′+4′, the coupled 8′+8′+4′, the buff), the organ's registration (the flutes of a second manual, the principals, the plenum: D349's model, so the pedal follows), and the consoles' own moves (a pulse's width on CLASSIC CHIP, GAME BOY and SID 64, MEGA FM's FM index, a send into a sound's own echo, hall or church, VOCAL SYNTH's vowel, the piano's soft layer, the mallets' hardness, a filter's veil). A moment may be for some voices only (Bodky's right hand on the softer keyboard). **Rules** (`test/d355-colour-plans.test.ts`): the band only (`Play.colour`, set by `proto-game.ts` from the note's onset and voice; `Chip.note` colours a band note, never the player's); the player's own stops win (`bandRegistration`: the plan's stops for the band unless the player's TUTTI registers everything); no moment has a level, the organ takes at most two ranks, a moment for some voices may only soften or veil them (never a louder subject); each note reads its colour where it starts, so a sounding note keeps its stop, and the moments sit at cadences, episodes and section boundaries; on a phone the bass takes no lowpass, buff, darker shelf or pulse width (`bandColour`); nothing is judged. A plan is the band's whole colour from its moment (`{}`: the sound's own). The pop-up shows "♪ THIS SOUND HAS ITS OWN COLOURS HERE" (pt ESTE SOM TEM CORES PRÓPRIAS AQUI, es ESTE SONIDO TIENE SUS PROPIOS COLORES AQUÍ) when the sound has a plan in the piece, on the friends' page too. **2. Book I's fugues** (stage 1): every fugue a sound of its own, replacing D288's CLASSIC CHIP for all, and plans for the HARPSICHORD and PIPE ORGAN in all 24 plus two or three other sounds each (the piece's own among them): 24 pieces, 332 moments. Sources: Bodky, *The Interpretation of Bach's Keyboard Works* (1960), whose harpsichord registrations by bar (as extracted in docs/wtc/readings, D272) are followed in I/2, 4, 5, 7, 10, 11, 15, 16, 20, 21, 22, except where he brings the subject out on the louder keyboard (I/10, I/21: left out, the owner's rule); Keller (1965/1976) for character and his shading by expositions (I/8) and his contrast of subject and interludes (I/12); Ledbetter (2002) for the sections and pedal points (data/wtc/ledbetter-sections.json); Booth, "Controlling dynamics on the harpsichord", *Harpsichord & Fortepiano* 26/1 (2021), checked: forte and piano in Baroque keyboard music mostly meant a change of manual, Bach asking for a two-manual harpsichord in only a few works (the Italian Concerto, the French Overture's Echo); for the organ, Bach marks manual changes only exceptionally (the "Dorian" Toccata BWV 538, checked) and Stauffer has argued against manual changes in the free works (known here only through a review of Faulkner, *The Registration of J. S. Bach's Organ Works*, 2008: not checked in Stauffer): the organ plans are few moments, mostly the plenum for a final stretto or pedal, and are a modern player's choice. The sounds: I/1, 4, 17 PIPE ORGAN; I/2, 5, 16, 20, 21 HARPSICHORD; I/6, 8, 14, 18 PIANO; I/7 SWITCHED-ON (Carlos recorded it, 1968); I/3 GAME BOY; I/9 SUPER 16-BIT; I/10 CLASSIC CHIP; I/11 BLEEPS AND BLOOPS; I/12, 24 NOCTURNE 32-BIT; I/13 TOMITA; I/15 MEGA FM; I/19 TRACKER; I/22 SOLO VOICES (Keller's "full, warm choral sound", five voices in singers' compass); I/23 VOCAL SYNTH; each reason in `tools/touch/colour-plans/book1.ts`. **3. Book II's fugues** (stage 2, `book2.ts`), read the same way (Bodky's right hand where his hands agree; Keller's gavotte episodes of II/13 "two bars forte, two bars piano", placed in episode 1 and its return, a reading of him): II/2, 7, 22 PIPE ORGAN; II/3, 6, 13, 16 HARPSICHORD; II/4, 14, 18 PIANO; II/5 VOCAL SYNTH (the Swingle Singers sang it); II/9 SOLO VOICES (Keller's Palestrina style; four voices in singers' compass); II/8, 19 NOCTURNE 32-BIT (II/8 among Mozart's string-quartet arrangements, K.405); II/1 MEGA FM; II/10 SID 64; II/11 CLASSIC CHIP; II/12 BLEEPS AND BLOOPS; II/15 TRACKER; II/17, 21 SUPER 16-BIT; II/20 SWITCHED-ON; II/23 TOMITA; II/24 GAME BOY: 48 fugues, 620 moments. **4. The Art of Fugue and the ricercars** (stages 3-4, `aof.ts`, read from the encoding and Bach's titles): C1, 7, 11 and the Ricercar a 6 PIPE ORGAN (Gould's organ Contrapuncti 1-9, 1962, checked; "per Augmentationem"); C2, 6 ("in Stylo Francese"), 13 rectus and inversus (Bach's own two-harpsichord arrangement) and 14 HARPSICHORD; C3 and the Ricercar a 3 PIANO (Bach improvised it on Frederick's Silbermann fortepiano, 1747); C4 NOCTURNE 32-BIT; C5 SUPER 16-BIT; C8 SID 64; C9 VOCAL SYNTH (the Swingle Singers, 1963); C10 TRACKER; C12 SOLO VOICES; the canons CLASSIC CHIP, GAME BOY, BLEEPS AND BLOOPS, SWITCHED-ON. **5. MORE BACH's others** (`more.ts`): the Inventions and Sinfonias stay on the HARPSICHORD ("Clavier", Bach's title page), but the three Carlos recorded (F, B♭, D minor) on SWITCHED-ON and the slow chromatic Invention 9 and Sinfonias 5 and 9 on the PIANO (the title page's "cantabile manner of playing"); each with harpsichord and organ plans and one console's moment. BWV 1043 II, BWV 228's fugue and BWV 54's fugal aria keep their sounds with one moment each. The HARPSICHORD CONCERTOS have none: their solo and tutti are already in the orchestration. In all 103 pieces, 1026 moments. D297's rule that no WTC piece opens on the ten newer sounds gives way for these (tests D246, D249, D292, D297 follow). **Open for the owner's ears:** the organ defaults depend on D349's one organ; I/22 on SOLO VOICES; whether the 4′ (+0.5 dB, brighter) is enough of a terrace. |
| D356 | WTC 1.65. The owner: "WTC fugues also unlock tempo toggle when they're completed with A level." D351's TEMPO button (SLOW · DEFAULT · FAST, one button cycling, kept per piece in `tb-lite-tempo-v1`) on the WTC's 48 fugues. **The unlock**: in the friends' view (play.html, Book I; Book II when unlocked) a fugue's button appears once that fugue was finished with an A or better (A or S, lite.ts `tempoEarned` = D265's `soundUnlocked`: `best`, or a grade from before 1.52 in `bestOld`); a B, or a run the crowd left, does not open it. Before: nothing on a fugue never finished; on one finished without an A a single quiet line, AN A ON THIS FUGUE UNLOCKS TEMPO (pt UM A NESTA FUGA DESBLOQUEIA O ANDAMENTO, es UNA A EN ESTA FUGA DESBLOQUEA EL TEMPO), where the button would be. The unlocked view (tb-unlock-all) shows the button on every fugue; MORE BACH and CHORALES stay unlocked-view only; preludes have none. **Tempi**: D351's rules from the fugue's played tempo (tempi.json `normal`): SLOW 0.75×; FAST 1.25× capped where the busiest line reaches 5.5 notes/s, never under 1.12× (e.g. I/1 ♩ 37 · 49 · 61). Judging follows the tempo. **Sounds**: D351's Art of Fugue row: SLOW the PIPE ORGAN, DEFAULT the fugue's own (PIECE_SOUND read at runtime, so D355's tailored defaults apply unchanged), FAST CLASSIC CHIP; where the fugue's own sound is already the step's (today most fugues are on CLASSIC CHIP), FAST plays the HARPSICHORD instead, and SLOW the PIANO where the own sound is the PIPE ORGAN, so each step changes the sound. Applied in the friends' view too (where no sound is chosen); in the unlocked view a sound chosen for the fugue is kept at every tempo (as D351). **Bests**: a run at FAST (or DEFAULT) counts as any: its grade toward the fugue's best and the A that opens the tempo, its score toward the best score. A run at SLOW is kept as finished (its ✓, tiers) but its grade and score are kept apart (`slowBest`, `slowScores` in `tb-lite-v1`, lite.ts `recordLiteSlow`), never replacing the best grade or score; the result's best line compares it with the best at SLOW. (MORE BACH and CHORALES unchanged: every tempo counts, as D351.) **The result** shows the tempo played whenever the button was open: TEMPO · FAST · ♩ 61, at SLOW on a fugue + · KEPT APART FROM YOUR BEST (translated). Screenshots (iPhone 13 landscape, I/1's pop-up: never finished, B with the hint, A at DEFAULT/FAST/SLOW, the hint in PT, the unlocked view) in the session's scratchpad `v165/`. | `src/bench/tempo-steps.ts` (`isWtcFugue`, `tempoOpen`, `WTC_SOUND_ELSE`, kind `wtc`), `src/bench/lite.ts` (`tempoEarned`, `recordLiteSlow`, `slowBest`, `slowScores`), `src/bench/BenchApp.tsx`, `bench.css`; `src/i18n/pt.ts`, `es.ts`; `test/d356-wtc-tempo.test.ts`, `d351-tempo-steps.test.ts` | — |
| D357 | WTC 1.71. PIPE ORGAN, D349's follow-ups (the owner loved D349: "every single example is DRAMATICALLY better"). **1. The stops one touch away** (the owner: "the instrument stops should not be folded on a box, they should be readily available upon a single touch. this is the only way one can, amidst play, change stops"). D322's buttons are now one row of one-touch toggles in the music box's title bar, always there while the harpsichord or the organ plays. The STOPS fold and the long press are withdrawn, and so is D343's hiding: the row shows for the friends whenever the sound is one of the two (full or playing); Book I's fugues play the chip sound, so they show none yet. The row sits between the thumbs' bars, never on them. Each button is 30 px drawn and 46 px tall to the touch, a lit knob when drawn and an empty, sunken one when pushed in. TUTTI is a button of its own (D322's semantics: every voice takes the player's stops), and on the organ ROOM sits beside it (2). Where the bar is narrow (a phone sideways) the labels shorten (PRINC., MIXT.), the font goes to 6 px, the piece's title gives way and the key stays. On iPhone 13 landscape the organ's seven buttons, ❚❚ and ✕ fit within the box. A phone upright still shows none. **The owner's note for later:** "coordinating stop changes will be relevant to some special challenges." Every change made at the row while playing is kept for the run with its bar (`recordStopChange`: bar, stop or TUTTI, drawn or not, the registration after; cleared when a run starts). A challenge can ask "draw X at bar N" (`StopCue` {bar, stop, drawn, early}) and check it (`cueMet`). Nothing scores it yet. `onLiveRegistration` tells the row of a change made elsewhere (for D355's band registration events: the row redraws when the live registration changes). **2. ROOM** (the owner: "i did miss a bit of the reverb of an organ. is it something that one can control via hovering? ... it's nice to be able to play clean and dry like this. but sometimes one wants [more]"). A touch screen has no hover, so this is one button cycling DRY · CHURCH · CATHEDRAL, kept on the device (`tg-organ-room-v1`, every piece), from the next note: the old room's input is closed and its tail rings out (room.ts; chip.ts `fxInput`/`period`). CHURCH, the default, is D349's church exactly (RT60 2.6 s; 1.7 s on a phone). DRY: 1.3 s (1.0 s on a phone), sent at 0.6. CATHEDRAL: 4.2 s (3.0 s on a phone, impulse 2.6 s), sent 3.5 dB more, its send high-passed at 160 Hz (220 Hz on a phone, a new `Reverb.phoneHp`), so the pedal's line stays in the direct sound. **Measured** (I/2, the music cut at bar 15, the tail 0.2-0.7 s after it against the music's last 2 s): the phone heard much less room than a computer. CHURCH's tail is -23 dB on a computer and -30 dB on a phone (through the speaker approximation), which is probably the "a bit dry". CATHEDRAL gives -15 on a computer and -19 on a phone; DRY about -38 and -45. The pedal's band (40-250 Hz) is no wetter than the whole: CATHEDRAL -17 against -15 on a computer, -20 against -19 on a phone. **3. The plenum's bass cutting out at the start** (the owner: "something wrong with the plenum: the bass in the beginning is cutting for some weird reason"). Reproduced on D349's i2-reg-plenum: the pedal's detached sixteenths in the subject (0.11 s held) came out 13 dB under their neighbours (per note, -46 dB against -31 to -36), so the bass dropped in and out while it played detached at the start, then steadied once it moved in legato. Not loading, not voice stealing (0 stolen, phone included), and not the pedal/manual switch at C4 (2-3 dB). Two causes, from D349 meeting the recorded pipes. (a) D349's "a joined note's pipe speaks anew" (`piped`: legato off) made every recorded pedal note start at its recording's very beginning (before, a joined note entered 0.25 s in), and the VCSL pipes take 55-120 ms (pedal) and 20-100 ms (plenum) to reach half their level (measured). Now a recorded pipe enters its recording near where it speaks: `Loaded.speech` is measured at decode (the time to half the steady level), and `SampledInstrument.speak` (0.02 s of the speech kept, at most `SPEAK_MAX` 0.12 s in) is set for `organPedal` and `organPleno` only. (b) D304's early release of a detached recorded note (begun 30 ms in for a 0.11 s note) no longer applies to organ pipes (`Patch.pipe`), which are let go at their written end as the synthesized ones are (D349: the phrasing's hold is the organist's release). Those sixteenths now measure -31 to -32 against their neighbours' -28 to -34. (c) Found on the way: D349 took the recorded pedal for 16′ and 8′, but measured it sounds at 8′ (C3: the octave below 59 dB under the fundamental; nothing under 100 Hz in the plenum render at all, -75 dB). So the plenum had no 16′, and none under the bass while the subject sat in the tenor's register. `orgPedalPleno` now carries a synthesized Subbass 16′ (`Patch.under`, a new field: a rank an octave below, sounding as one note with it, `together`). The player's 16′ button pushes it in (`PEDAL_PLENO_8`); the stand-in's own 16′ is withdrawn. The plenum's pedal now measures -25.7 LUFS, against the principal pedal's -26.5 (it was -28.3), and the whole plenum render is +1.5 dB (+1.0 on a phone). ORGANO PLENO likewise. **4. The Trompete** (the owner: "i love principal + trompete … there's a je ne sais quoi to the organ trumpet sound which i love… like cotton candy to my ears"). The reed's speech and body (`Patch.reed`, chip.ts `reedBody`): the pitch speaks 14 cents flat and settles in about 20 ms; the tongue rattles on the shallot for the first 35 ms (the level beaten at 38 Hz, a square LFO); the attack is brighter at once (the lowpass opened 2.2-fold, closing over 40 ms, was 1.6/30 ms); the resonator's formant is a peak at 1.4 kHz (+4 dB, Q 1.1); the compass is fuller low and thinner high (a low shelf at twice the note, +2.5 dB at C2 to -4 dB at C6); no vibrato. Its second rank (+1.2 cents) and its -0.9 cents against the principal still beat slowly. In the chorus (`organPatch`) the reed's speech and body are carried into the merged note at its share (0.5 with the Principal); before, the principal's envelope took them over. Its ranks are 1.5 dB lower, so it is not louder: I/2's soprano and alto on the reed -27.1 and -28.0 LUFS (were -26.8 and -28.2), Principal + Trompete -26.0 and -26.7 (were -26.0 and -26.6). The A/B renders are within ±0.1 dB, the spectrum moved from 2.5-6 kHz (-0.8 to -1.0 dB) into 0.8-2.5 kHz (+0.4 to +0.9). Organ reed recordings under CC0/CC BY were looked for: VCSL has only flue ranks for organs (the Pipe Organ's loud and quiet manuals and pedals, the Renaissance Organ's 8′, 4′ and full), so the reed stays synthesized. An A/B page (local, scratchpad) covers the plenum (I/2 whole and pedal alone, ORGANO PLENO, II/9), the reed alone and Principal + Trompete (a chorale cantus firmus, I/2, II/9), the three rooms (a chorale, I/2; computer and phone renders) and screenshots of the stop row on iPhone 13 landscape. **Open for the owner's ears:** the rattle and bloom amounts; CATHEDRAL's length on a phone; the plenum's synthesized 16′ against the "cool" recorded pedal. **API for D355:** `setLiveRegistration` now notifies `onLiveRegistration` listeners; new exports `PEDAL_PLENO_8`, `stopChanges`/`recordStopChange`/`clearStopChanges`/`cueMet`, types `StopChange`/`StopCue`/`StopKnob`; `RegistrationStops` takes `barNow`; `setTutti`/`toggle` unchanged; `TUTTI_HOLD_MS` withdrawn. | `src/touch/RegistrationStops.tsx`, `registration.ts`, `room.ts` (new), `chip.ts` (`fxInput` by room, `period`, `reedBody`, `Patch.under` in `note`, `speakFrom`, the detached release), `sampled.ts` (`speechOf`, `Loaded.speech`, `speak`, `SPEAK_MAX`, `speakFrom`), `sound.ts` (`orgTrompete`, `orgPedalPleno`, `Patch.under`, `Patch.reed`), `touch.css`; `src/bench/BenchApp.tsx` (the row while playing, for the friends too; `barNow`; the changes cleared at a run's start); i18n pt, es; `test/d357-organ.test.ts` | WTC 1.67+ |
| D358 | WTC 1.71 (approved by the owner). The owner on D349's organ: "every single example is DRAMATICALLY better. please try to get some general knowledge from what you did there, because it may improve lots of presets, or, even more promisingly, form the basis of new ones." **The principles** written down in `docs/playback/SOUND-PRINCIPLES.md`: decide what a preset claims to be (one instrument, one ensemble, or a band of unlike instruments on purpose, as the consoles are); one instrument, one patch; one room, one send; placement by key (a keyboard) or by a seating plan (an ensemble), narrow; one envelope per family; timbre by register, never by voice index; the player's notes the band's (never louder or brighter, the owner's rule); articulation in the instrument's own way (release, over-legato, bow change, consonant); per-key fixed micro-tuning, drift only for analog synthesizers; timing scatter by the number of players; on a phone, a bass reinforced by upper partials. **The audit** (each voice rendered alone through the game's Chip: gated loudness, centroid by register, left-right balance, balance through the phone filter; I/2, II/9, I/22, BWV 1052/i, BWV 1043/i): the harpsichord and the piano were four instruments (voices at ±0.45: 10-13 dB of left-right spread between voices, wider than any other preset; a bass patch +1.9 dB (harpsichord) or +1.6 dB (piano) with three quarters of the room, the piano's inner voices on a patch 1 dB lighter; the player's notes 1 dB louder and 2.5 dB brighter); SOLO VOICES sang five syllables at once with FluidR3's synth choir as its second of five (4.3 dB loudness spread in I/22) and five sends; the concertos' band had no seating (the three string lines right of centre, the harpsichord left) and a bassoon continuo; TRACKER's slapped bass stood 4-7 dB over the violin on a computer and through the phone filter. The consoles and synthesizer records are bands on purpose and keep their colours; CLASSIC CHIP (pans ±0.45, the triangle 10-13 dB over the pulses on a computer) and VOCAL SYNTH (the soprano 4-7 dB under the mezzo and the tenor) proposed only. **Changed** (`sound.ts`, `chip.ts`, `sampled.ts`): `Patch.one` (one instrument or ensemble: no MINE lift and `leadOf` unchanged, `chip.ts` `oneInstrument`: placement by key (`span`, `lo`, `hi`), fixed per-key cents, a lute's courses as `Play.choirs`), `Patch.bow` (a bowed string's joined note a new stroke: the recording entered 60 ms after its onset, rising over 30 ms, where a slur skips 0.25 s; `STRING_BOW`), both additive in a separated block of `chip.ts`. HARPSICHORD and PIANO one patch for every voice (`oneKeyboard`: every voice at 0), placed by key (`HARPSI_ONE` ±0.15 over A1-F6, ±0.8 cent; `PIANO_ONE` ±0.2 over the keyboard, ±0.5 cent), one send (harpsiBassSampled, pianoBassSampled and pianoInnerSampled kept, no longer played); measured: voice loudness spread 4.1-4.6 dB to 0.6-0.7 dB (I/2), voice pan spread 9.8-12.7 dB to 1.4-5.8 dB (the piano's recordings' own image is erratic per sampled key, ±5 dB: a fix would need the samples re-imaged). SOLO VOICES one ensemble: one syllable ("la"), one send (`VOICE_SEND` 0.15), one envelope (in 30 ms, out 0.15 s), a row (soprano left to bass right, within ±0.2), a soprano (`singS2`) for the second of five and six; credit VocalSet alone. SUPER 16-BIT's concertos and BWV 1043 seated (`SEATS`: the soloists in front in the middle, the first violins left, the seconds right, the violas right of centre, the continuo in the middle), the continuo a cello (`s16Cello`, a new set `cello16` from the Freesound cello, tuning and lengths measured), bow changes on its violin, strings and cello; NOCTURNE 32-BIT's concerto band on the same plan. TRACKER's bass 3.5 dB lighter (0.6 → 0.4). **New, not offered** (`TRIALS`: presetById finds them, the players' list does not): STRING CONSORT (two violins, viola, cello one to a part: the VSO2 violin, VSCO 2 CE's violas, the Freesound cello at 24 kHz; seated as a quartet, the salon, one send, bow changes; Vallotti at A = 415) and LUTE (the classical guitar's recordings with a lute's courses: unisons above G3, octave strings below; placed by key, ±1.5 cent frets; played as the harpsichord, `phrasing.ts` `styleOf`; equal at A = 415); credits `CONSORT_CREDIT`, `LUTE_CREDIT`. Proposed, not built: a baroque oboe band (two oboes, taille, bassoon: VSCO's oboe and the bassoon, CC0 and CC BY), a clavichord with Bebung (synthesized), a recorder consort (synthesized: the flute recordings are a modern flute). An A/B page (local, scratchpad presets/) has each changed preset before and after on one or two passages, computer and phone, and the new presets. **Open for the owner's ears:** key placement or dead centre for the keyboards; the piano's balance without D294's voicing patches; one syllable for the singers; the cello continuo, antiphonal violins and the bow change; whether the consoles' player lift should go too; the trials. Note for D357's merge: `chip.ts` `sampledNote`'s `from` line is changed by both (D357's `speakFrom`, D358's `bow.skip`). `test/d358-sound-principles.test.ts`; D292, D297, D302 and D348 tests updated. |
| D359 | WTC 1.69. **THE MANUAL AUDITED AND FIXED** (the owner: "audit the tutorial and try to improve it to masterclass level. the tutorial ought to be so good that it will be memorable"). Phase A (D342) lived as a first-time player would, on play.html at iPhone 13 landscape in Playwright (a frozen build of WTC 1.65), each lesson driven by Herr Bach's own hands and by random taps, and every pass rule run in node by simulated players: Herr Bach, a careful player (on time ±15 ms, holds 0.8), near misses (60 ms early, 60 ms late, scattered ±90 ms, one thumb, detached, every fifth note the wrong way) and a random tapper (test/d359-players.ts, d342-sim.ts). **The audit, ranked** (the report in the session's scratchpad `manual2/`): (1) the organ lessons (I.4, I.5, II.3) sounded on the harpsichord: their preset id was the retired sampled organ (RETIRED maps it to the harpsichord); (3) THE COACH (manual/coach.ts): every COUNTS line carries its fix, and a take that falls short is answered with the first unmet line's, in musical terms, with the bar it happened in, from the lens's new per-note record (`LensStats.per`: bar, beat, right/wrong/missed, the way the thumb went and the line's, offset, thumb, release word, join, gesture, held into a rest): "Bar 18: you held into the rest. Lift as the note ends.", "Bar 2: the line falls; your thumb went up. Lower when it falls.", "Bar 4: a leap. Move your thumb further up: a leap must look like one.", "Early by 62 ms, most in bar 2. Let the other voice bring the note to you.", "No thumb rested early. As one thumb strikes, put the other on its next note; lift the first on the beat."; the lens sheet stays under it. (4) Each stage introduced by what it is for, in his words, one line (`Stage.say`), with the material named small beneath (`StageLine`) and PLAY; after his demonstration straight to the first stage. (8) THE ARC: a checkpoint passed seals its part: a wax seal pressed with the part's numeral and German title (`Seal`), his line for the part (I: "So. The strike is yours. Each run now puts the game's wall on its fake audio clock (`g.wall = () => clock.t * 1000`, D317's hook) and stamps with `g.wall()`: the same touches, the same verdicts, however busy the machine. Shown: the full suite three times in a row while `npx vite build` ran in a loop beside it (load about 25 on 4 cores). Bach, Versuch I.2.2 §9; the report's table: a single note on a strong beat, a beat or more long, the note before single, joined to it and a diatonic step away), the grace is ARMED: the held note sounds on over the beat as the appoggiatura (MECH.holdOverSound TIED, a suspension, the default; STRUCK AGAIN re-attacks it there, necessarily about 70 ms late, to compare), and the note is not missed while the lift may come. A's lift before MECH.holdOverShare (75%) of the note (holdOverFermataShare, 90%, on a fermata or the last note) sounds it by the pull-off, unjudged for time (right and PERFECT, the flash APPOGGIATURA); the note held over judged HELD at its written end, the join from the lift; later than that, the note is missed as without it. | D368 | WTC 1.73. The owner: "When I told you I wanted subject color to change, I meant some kind of hue modifier to the voice original colour, and not some change to the same colour in every voice as it plays the subject. You did the latter and now shit is confusing." D352 misread the request: it bent every voice's subject notes toward one primary per subject (red, blue, yellow), so different voices playing the subject looked alike. Now each voice keeps its own colour and only that colour is modified while it states a subject: the first subject brighter and fuller in the voice's own hue; the second and third turned ±34° off it (so a double or triple fugue's subjects stay apart within each voice); the rim, the shaded band, the tag and the embers a pale glow of that same voice's subject colour (subject-hue.ts subjectInk, subjectRim). D352's raise of a non-player voice during its entry, the membership and the fades are kept. |

## The chorale mode (C series)

The chorale mode's decisions are numbered in a series of their own (C1, C2, ...), so that they never
collide with the game's D numbers, which run on another branch (owner, after the Choices-lab session
found D98-D102 used twice).

| id | decision | effect in code |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | Owner: **Bach is the last word.** In the chorale mode, Bach's own four-part chorales are the authority: where Kittel (or any theorist) and Bach disagree, Bach's practice decides what counts as correct; Kittel's basses are the model of the figured-bass method and of the many-basses-for-one-melody exercise, read against Bach. | `docs/chorales/PLAN.md` |
| C2 | Owner: chorale sources. Bach: the 371 Breitkopf chorales in Craig Sapp's Humdrum edition (370 files; no. 150 is not four-part), CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, vendored at a pinned commit; the derived data carry the same licence. Kittel: *Vierundzwanzig Choräle mit acht verschiedenen Bässen* (1811), from the engraved PDF (public domain, owner), read from its vector glyphs, not by optical recognition. The chorale mode starts as a library (parsed notes, then analysis), with no generator until the principles are understood (owner: "start slow"). | `tools/chorales/`, `data/chorales/`, `data/sources/bach-370-chorales/`, `data/sources/kittel-24/` |
| C3 | Owner: Kirnberger is the authority for the analytical categories of the chorale library (*Die Kunst des reinen Satzes*, 1771-79; with Schulz, *Die wahren Grundsätze*, 1773): two fundamental chords (triad, seventh chord) on a fundamental bass, essential and incidental dissonance. Until his text can be read (no access from the build environment), the categories are applied as operational tests marked for checking, with no quotation or page given. | `tools/chorales/kirnberger.py`, `docs/chorales/KIRNBERGER.md` |
| C4 | Owner: the chorale mode has a conception of its own, not an extension of the Fux mode (`docs/chorales/CONCEPT.md`). A harmonization is trained level by level, top-down: plan (cadences), harmony (fundamental bass), bass, figures, voices (strict style), diminution (free style). Solutions are judged by their place among Bach's and Kittel's choices (idiom with examples, comparison with the masters' settings); only a few boundaries, chosen by the owner from what Bach virtually never does, are errors. Fux is the prerequisite for basic voice-leading. | `docs/chorales/CONCEPT.md`, `tools/chorales/cadence_plans.py` |
| C5 | Owner ("ya" to the proposals): the chorale mode has its own screen ("Chorales" in the voices selector). Level 1 is played on the melody alone, with a marker under each fermata for the cadence chord (Roman numerals of the chorale's key). Compare lays each choice beside Kittel's basses, Bach's settings of the tune and Bach's habit in the same context, with links to the Breitkopf 1784 print; markers turn green (with Bach), blue (with Kittel only) or amber (neither), and nothing is marked wrong. Playback: the melody, with its small notes as passing notes, and a plain chord on each chosen cadence. Modal chorales stay, as a later stage. Assumed until the owner says otherwise: Kittel is a second reference at every level; figures will be both typed and chosen from a palette. | `src/ui/ChoraleApp.tsx`, `src/ui/notation/MelodyScore.tsx`, `src/chorale/level1.ts`, `data/chorales/level1.json` |
| C6 | Owner: **the Well-Tempered Clavier is the end goal** ("everything is Bach"). Assumed overnight, for the owner to confirm: the WTC mode gets its own screen ("Well-Tempered Clavier" in the voices selector); its first level is Prelude 1's harmonic plan (choose the chord of each bar from four over Bach's bass, all from Bach's own vocabulary in the prelude, heard in his figuration, compared bar by bar with Bach's chord, figures and fundamental; nothing marked wrong). Conception: preludes first (harmony laid out in time), then fugues (counterpoint against a subject), levels in `docs/wtc/PLAN.md`. | `src/ui/PreludeApp.tsx`, `src/wtc/prelude1.ts`, `data/wtc/prelude1.json`, `tools/wtc/prelude1.py`, `docs/wtc/PLAN.md` |
| C7 | WTC sources (assumed, owner to confirm): the Humdrum edition's notes stay local (`data/local/`, git-ignored: its rights notices reserve derivative electronic formats), and only facts derived from them are committed (subjects as pitch names, entries by bar, counts). Notes the game plays come from public-domain editions (Mutopia; Prelude 1 vendored and checked against Humdrum, 32/32 bars). Human Roman-numeral analyses from When in Rome (CC BY-SA 4.0) are the yardstick for the harmonic reader, fetched locally; only agreement figures are committed. | `tools/wtc/fetch_wtc.sh`, `tools/wtc/fetch_wir.sh`, `data/wtc/sources/mutopia/`, `.gitignore` |
| C8 | WTC level F2, the answer (assumed overnight, owner to confirm): for each fugue whose subject the analysis delimits with confidence (26 of 48), the player hears the subject and chooses the answer among the real one, the textbook tonal one (the dominant at the head answered by the tonic) and Bach's where it is neither; options are lettered in an order that does not reveal whose is whose; Compare names Bach's, its kind, and the notes changed against the real answer. Bach's answers: 20 real, 18 textbook tonal, 10 tonal of his own (`docs/wtc/ANSWERS.md`). | `src/ui/AnswerApp.tsx`, `src/wtc/answers.ts`, `src/ui/notation/LineStaff.tsx`, `tools/wtc/answers.py`, `data/wtc/answers.json` |
| C9 | WTC level P2, Prelude 1's figured bass (assumed overnight, owner to confirm): the bass and Bach's figures are given, printed as a continuo part prints them (the common chord unfigured, characteristic numbers only, an accidental before the number it alters, alone for the third; the convention of D101). For each bar the player voices the chord in the prelude's layout (tenor and three right-hand notes) from up to six voicings that all contain the figured notes. Compare sets the voicing beside Bach's, says how far the voices move, and marks parallel fifths and octaves from the bar before as faults, since in the figuration each of the five notes is a line. Bach's prelude has none, so the boundary is Bach's as well as Fux's. | `src/ui/PreludeApp.tsx`, `src/wtc/prelude1.ts` (`parallels`, `motion`), `tools/wtc/prelude1.py` (`figured`, `voicings`) |
| C10 | Chorale level 2, the harmony (assumed overnight, owner to confirm): on the same screen, a level selector (1 cadence plan, 2 harmony); at level 2 a marker under every written melody note, the player choosing its chord (Roman numerals of the chorale's key) from the commonest chords in Bach's habit at that melody degree plus every chord Bach or Kittel uses there; Compare as at level 1, note by note (Kittel's basses, Bach's settings of the tune aligned note by note, Bach's habit with examples); playback puts a plain chord under each chosen note. The habit measured held out by tune: 58% first, 87% in the first three (docs/chorales/LEVEL2.md). | `src/ui/ChoraleApp.tsx`, `src/chorale/level2.ts`, `tools/chorales/harmony_plans.py`, `data/chorales/level2.json` |
| C11 | Chorale level 3, the bass (assumed overnight, owner to confirm): under every melody note the player chooses the bass as a scale degree of the chorale's key (it sounds in the octave nearest the bass before); options from Bach's habit at that melody degree plus every degree Bach or Kittel uses there; Compare as at levels 1-2. Kittel's book is this level (eight basses under one melody). The habit, held out by tune: melody degree and bass before rank Bach's bass first 48%, in the first three 82% (docs/chorales/LEVEL3.md). | `src/ui/ChoraleApp.tsx`, `src/chorale/level3.ts`, `tools/chorales/bass_plans.py`, `data/chorales/level3.json` |
| C12 | Chorale level 4, the figures (assumed overnight, owner to confirm): one of Kittel's basses (selector [1]-[9]) shown unfigured on a bass staff; under every bass note the player chooses the chord's position (5/3, 6, 6/4, 7, 6/5, 4/3, 4/2); compared with Kittel's own chord there (his printed figure beside it: the key of the exercise) and Bach's habit over that bass degree under that melody degree; playback realises the figure in the key. Habit held out by tune: 81% first, 98% in the first three; Bach's commonest position is Kittel's at 75% of his bass notes (docs/chorales/LEVEL4.md). | `src/ui/ChoraleApp.tsx`, `src/chorale/level4.ts`, `src/ui/notation/MelodyScore.tsx` (bass clef), `tools/chorales/figure_plans.py`, `data/chorales/level4.json` |
| C13 | WTC level P1 extended to the other figuration preludes of Book I (assumed overnight, owner to confirm): 1/2, 1/5 and 1/6, from the public-domain Mutopia editions read by `tools/wtc/lily.py` and checked against Humdrum (35/38, 34/35, 25/26 bars identical; the rest editorial readings). A figured bar is the prelude's commonest bar pattern filled with pitches; the player chooses its right hand (Bach's, or a neighbouring bar's over this bar's left hand); bars outside the pattern sound as written. Compare gives Bach's right hand and the fundamental of each. | `src/ui/PlanApp.tsx`, `src/wtc/preludes.ts`, `tools/wtc/prelude_plans.py`, `data/wtc/preludes/` |
| C14 | The owner's guidance relayed by the main session (9 Oct, late): the WTC mode is first a study guide ("access to all pieces ... listen to sections, best moments, all subject entries, one voice in a distinctive sound ... exercise is the least of our concern"), built by the main session (`src/ui/WtcStudy.tsx`, D119-D123). Consequence here: this branch's WTC screens are renamed from the mode "wtc" to "preludes" ("WTC · preludes and harmony", strings in the `wtcp` section), so that both can stand side by side after a merge; its fugue-answer level overlaps the main session's D120 and the lab's level 2 and should give way to them at the merge (owner's call). This branch's lasting contributions to the study guide are meant to be the data and readers: the public-domain Mutopia notes and their reader, the harmonic reader validated against human analyses, the figuration measure, the three-way cross-check. | `src/ui/Root.tsx`, `src/content/en.json` |
| C15 | Owner ("go ahead" with the plan after the chorale-mode assessment): Bach's settings laid open, the conception's first kind of task ("hear and read", CONCEPT.md section 5.1), before any production. A view of the chorale screen, first in its level menu ("Bach's settings · hear and read"): any of the 370 chorales (or only the 77 settings of Kittel's 22 tunes), Bach's four voices as a page of systems (four bars a row, the row playing kept in sight; the upbeat bar drawn whole), heard voice by voice (each can be switched off), with four layers switched on at will: the **cadences** over the fermatas (PAC, IAC, HC, Phrygian HC, DC, and the bass's final degree), the **figures** derived from Bach's voices stacked under the bass as a continuo part prints them, the **fundamental bass** (root and kind of chord, Kirnberger's reading as the library applies it, C3), and the **incidental notes** coloured by kind (passing, neighbour, suspension, retardation, appoggiatura, anticipation, escape, free), with a legend and this chorale's counts. A phrase list with the library's cadence classes and a link to the Breitkopf 1784 print. The screen says that everything over Bach's notes is the library's reading, not his text. Data compacted for the single-file page (1.86 MB; loaded only when the view opens on the site). Nothing asked, nothing judged. | `tools/chorales/study_data.py`, `data/chorales/study.json`; `src/ui/ChoraleStudy.tsx`; `ChoraleApp.tsx` (level 0, the levels' screen as `ChoraleLevels`); `notation/WtcScore.tsx` (fermatas; text lines under the staves at onsets; rows with them); `test/chorale-study.test.ts` |
| C16 | Owner: the chorale mode has one style, Bach's; no strict/free setting. The Fux mode is the game's strict discipline and the prerequisite; the chorale mode is by nature less prohibition-based (C4). The discipline a strict setting would have given (above all the treatment of the seventh, which Fux does not teach) lies instead in the fineness of the habit's contexts: Bach's choices counted by approach, bass motion, voice and metric position, so that a choice he almost never makes in that context reads as such, with his few cases, and one he often makes reads as idiom; Kirnberger's categories name what is shown. **Boundaries** (the only errors): consecutive fifths and octaves on successive beats (Bach: one, no. 320 bar 8, to be checked in the Breitkopf print; 17 more through off-beat notes, not errors) and the augmented second in a line (none in 82,424 melodic steps). The unprepared seventh is not a boundary (354 of 2,335 sevenths on the beat enter free); the 364 sevenths that do not resolve down by step are to be examined one by one before anything is said of them. | `docs/chorales/CONCEPT.md` sections 1-4, 6, 7 |
| C17 | Owner ("go on" after the level-5 design): level 5, the voices. Over one of Kittel's figured basses (his eight or nine to each of 24 melodies: 194 exercises) and under the melody, the player writes the alto (upper staff) and the tenor (lower staff), one note to each of Kittel's harmonies (9,073 slots in all), by tapping the staff; ♭ ♮ ♯ alter the selected note. "Check" judges what is written as C16 says: **errors** only for consecutive fifths or octaves from chord to chord in a pair of voices with the alto or tenor (the given soprano and bass are not the player's), by similar motion, and for an augmented second in an inner line; **the figure** as the task (a letter the figure asks for that no voice has; an inner note outside the chord; a missing fifth only noted, as Bach often leaves it out); **habit** where the choice is one Bach makes fewer than one time in twenty in the same context, with his count and examples and his commonest choice beside it: doubling (by triad kind and the bass's member), the seventh in an inner voice (by voice, prepared or free, bass moving or held; its continuation read at the next change of chord), the leading tone of a dominant whose root falls a fifth (by voice), spacing; voices crossing or out of range are noted. Counts over Bach's 370 chorales (`tools/chorales/voice_plans.py`), e.g. a root-position major triad doubles the root in 86%; a prepared alto seventh with the bass moving falls by step in 80%. The score as a page of rows fitted to the width (two bars a row on a phone, four on a wider screen; the Bach study view too), empty slots drawn where their voice lives. Not yet: Bach's own inner voices beside the player's where he set the tune. | `tools/chorales/voice_plans.py`, `data/chorales/level5.json`; `src/chorale/level5.ts` (`judge`); `src/ui/ChoraleVoices.tsx`; `ChoraleApp.tsx` (level 5); `notation/WtcScore.tsx` (a tap goes to the staff tapped; `fitWidth`; slots' places); `notation/page-rows.ts`; `test/chorale-level5.test.ts` |
| C18 | Owner ("keep on working"): level 5 beside Bach. Where Bach set the tune (72 of his settings of 22 of Kittel's melodies, by the concordance), his setting is moved to Kittel's key (spelled: the letter distance of the first aligned pair, the semitones of the concordance's transposition) and aligned to Kittel's melody note by note (the concordance's alignment); at each matched melody note his alto, tenor and bass are kept. On the screen a selector shows and plays his setting (his bass with his inner voices, since they answer his bass, not Kittel's) in place of the player's voices over Kittel's bass. After Check, each setting is compared with what is written: how many of the places the player writes fall on a matched melody note, at how many of those Bach's bass is Kittel's, and there how often the player's alto and tenor sound his pitch classes, with the bars where they differ. Every lower voice keeps its length (all 1,110). The player taps a lower-voice note and chooses how it goes on to the next: a passing note (to a note a third away), an upper or lower neighbour (to the same note), a suspension (a step down into the next note, held over its first beat), an anticipation; only what the next note allows is offered, the added note in the last quaver, spelled by the signature unless that makes an augmented second. Of Bach's own embellishments, 80% are among the choices offered where he wrote them, and the note a choice makes is Bach's pitch in 91% (tested). Built first for Bach's settings in the study (a "Roman numerals" layer, on by default), from the local keys of C31 (the generator's applied chords read as V/x or vii°/x; diminished chords in lower case).
